, we call the simple
1
-edge tester
(\omega,\omega_{\iota},\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd})
with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd})
,
\phi
and
\mathcal{A}
(as defined in Definition
4.4
), the
edge tester for
\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi}(\mathcal{A})
.

Let us describe Definition
4.6
in words.
Suppose we are given an edge
\mathscrs{g}=\mathscrs{g}_{\circ}\cup\mathscrs{g}_{m}\in E(G[V_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}])
, where
\mathscrs{g}_{\circ}
contains the vertices of
\mathscrs{g}
that lie in clusters that are already embedded by
\phi
and
\mathscrs{g}_{m}
contains the remaining
m
vertices of
\mathscrs{g}
in the not yet embedded clusters with indices
I=\mathscrs{r}\setminus[r_{\circ}]
.
For each
H\in\mathcal{H}
, we track the set of vertices
\mathscrs{x}_{m}\in X_{\sqcup I}^{H}
where

•

\mathscrs{x}_{m}
still could be mapped onto
\mathscrs{g}_{m}
(that is,
\mathscrs{x}_{m}\cup\mathscrs{g}_{m}\in E(A_{I}^{H})
in (
4.8
)), and

•

\mathscrs{x}_{m}
lies in an
H
-edge
\mathscrs{e}
with
1^{st}
-pattern
\mathbf{p}
and
2^{nd}
-pattern
\mathbf{p}^{2nd}
such that if we map
\mathscrs{x}_{m}
onto
\mathscrs{g}_{m}
, then
\mathscrs{e}
is mapped onto
\mathscrs{g}
(that is,
(\phi|_{V(H)})^{-1}(\mathscrs{g}_{\circ})\cup\mathscrs{x}_{m}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\mathscrs{r})
in (
4.9
)).

Further, note that for a simple edge tester
(\omega,\omega_{\iota},\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd})
for
\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi}(\mathcal{A})
as in Definition
4.6
, we have that
\omega(E(\mathcal{A}_{I}))=|\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi}(\mathcal{A})|
for
I=\mathscrs{r}\setminus[r_{\circ}]
because the indicator function in (
4.6
) corresponds to (
4.8
), and the choice of
\mathscrs{x}
in (
4.6
) corresponds to (
4.9
) by the definition of
\omega_{\iota}
.

Next, we define in Definition
4.7
a set
E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi}(\mathcal{A})
for all distinct
G
-edges
\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}
with identical
G
-vertex in the last cluster such that
E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi}(\mathcal{A})
contains the tuples of
\mathcal{H}
-edges
(\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f})
with identical
\mathcal{H}
-vertex in the last cluster, and
\mathscrs{e}
and
\mathscrs{f}
can still be mapped onto
\mathscrs{g}
and
\mathscrs{h}
with respect to
\phi
and the candidacy graphs in
\mathcal{A}
.
In this case, we ignore the patterns as we only aim for an upper bound on the number of these edges and have some room to spare.

Definition 4.7

(
E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi}(\mathcal{A})
)
.

For edges
\mathscrs{g}=\{v_{i_{1}},\ldots,v_{i_{k}}\},\mathscrs{h}=\{w_{j_{1}},\ldots,w_{j_{k}}\}\in E(G)
with
v_{i_{k}}=w_{j_{k}}
and both
\mathscrs{z}\in\{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}\}
, let

\displaystyle E_{\mathscrs{z},\phi}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle:=E\left(\mathcal{H}\left[\phi^{-1}(\mathscrs{z}\cap V_{\cup[r_{\circ}]})\cup\textstyle\bigcup_{i\in[r]\setminus[r_{\circ}]}N_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}(\mathscrs{z}\cap V_{i})\right]\right);

\displaystyle E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle:=\left\{(\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f})\in E_{\mathscrs{g},\phi}(\mathcal{A})\times E_{\mathscrs{h},\phi}(\mathcal{A})\colon\mathscrs{e}\cap\mathscrs{f}\cap\mathscr{X}_{i_{k}}\neq\emptyset\right\}.

5.
Approximate Packing Lemma

In this section we provide our ‘Approximate Packing Lemma’ (Lemma
5.1
).
Given a blow-up instance
(\mathcal{H},G,R,\mathcal{X},\mathcal{V})
, it allows us for one cluster to embed almost all vertices of
\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X_{i}^{H}
into
V_{i}
, while maintaining crucial properties for future embedding rounds of other clusters.
To describe this setup we define a
packing instance
and collect some more notation.

5.1.
Packing instances

Our general understanding of a packing instance is as follows.
Recall that we will consider the clusters of a blow-up instance one after another.
A packing instance arises from a blow-up instance where we have already embedded vertices of some clusters (which is given by a function
\phi_{\circ}
)
and focuses only on one particular cluster (denoted by
\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X_{0}^{H}
and
V_{0}
)
and all clusters that are close to the considered cluster (measured in the reduced graph
R
).
We track
candidacy graphs
as defined in Definition
4.1
and consider a collection of candidacy graphs
\mathcal{A}
between the clusters in
\mathcal{H}
and
G
that will be used for future embedding rounds.
In order to be able to turn a partial packing into a complete one in the end, we do not only track the collection of candidacy graphs in
\mathcal{A}
but also a second collection of candidacy graphs
\mathcal{B}
, where the candidacy graphs in
\mathcal{B}
will be used for the completion step.
To that end, we also assume that the edges of
G
are partitioned into two parts
G_{A}
and
G_{B}
such that the edges in
G_{A}
are used for the approximate packing and the edges in
G_{B}
are reserved for the completion step.
That is, we will think of the graphs in
\mathcal{A}
as candidacy graphs with respect to
\phi_{\circ}
and
G_{A}
, and of the graphs in
\mathcal{B}
as candidacy graphs with respect to
\phi_{\circ}
and
G_{B}
.

We make this more precise.
Let
n,k,r,r_{\circ}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
We say
\mathscr{P}=(\mathcal{H},G_{A},G_{B},R,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\phi_{\circ}^{-},\phi_{\circ})
is a
packing instance of size
(n,k,r,r_{\circ})
if

•

\mathcal{H}
is a collection of
k
-graphs,
G_{A}
and
G_{B}
are edge-disjoint
k
-graphs on the same vertex set, and
R
is a
k
-graph where
V(R)=-[r_{\circ}]\cup[r]_{0}
;

•

\{X^{H}_{i}\}_{i\in{V(R)}}
is a vertex partition of
H\in\mathcal{H}
such that
|\mathscrs{e}\cap X_{i}^{H}|\leq 1
for all
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H)
,
H\in\mathcal{H}
;

•

\{V_{i}\}_{i\in{V(R)}}
is a vertex partition of
G_{A}
as well as
G_{B}
;

•

|X_{i}^{H}|=|V_{i}|=(1\pm 1/2)n
for each
i\in{V(R)}
;

•

for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
, the hypergraph
H[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]
is a matching if
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
and empty if
\mathscrs{r}\in\binom{V(R)}{k}\setminus E(R)
;

•

\mathcal{A}=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},I\subseteq[r]_{0}\colon\text{$I$ is an index set}}A^{H}_{I}
is a union of candidacy graphs with respect to
\phi_{\circ}
and
G_{A}
; in particular,
A^{H}_{I}
is
2|I|
-uniform, and
A^{H}_{i}
is a balanced bipartite
2
-graph with vertex partition
(X^{H}_{i},V_{i})
for each
i\in[r]_{0}
;

•

\mathcal{B}=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},j\in V(R)}B^{H}_{j}
is a union of candidacy graphs with respect to
\phi_{\circ}
and
G_{B}
; in particular,
B^{H}_{j}
is a balanced bipartite
2
-graph with vertex partition
(X^{H}_{j},V_{j})
for each
j\in{V(R)}
;

•

\phi_{\circ}^{-}\colon\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X^{H,-}_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}\to V_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}
with
X^{H,-}_{i}\subseteq X_{i}^{H}
,
\phi_{\circ}^{-}(X_{i}^{H,-})\subseteq V_{i}
and
\phi_{\circ}^{-}|_{X_{i}^{H,-}}
is injective for all
H\in\mathcal{H},i\in-[r_{\circ}]
, and

\phi_{\circ}\colon\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X^{H}_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}\to V_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}
is an extension of
\phi_{\circ}^{-}
with
\phi_{\circ}(X_{i}^{H})=V_{i}
and
\phi_{\circ}|_{X_{i}^{H}}
is bijective for all
H\in\mathcal{H},i\in-[r_{\circ}]
.

For simplicity, we often write
G:=G_{A}\cup G_{B}
,
\mathscr{X}_{i}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X^{H}_{i}
,
\mathscr{X}_{i}^{-}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X^{H,-}_{i}
,
\mathcal{A}_{I}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}A_{I}^{H}
,
\mathcal{B}_{i}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}B_{i}^{H}
,
\mathcal{X}_{\circ}:=\mathscr{X}_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}
,
\mathcal{X}_{\circ}^{-}:=\mathscr{X}_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}^{-}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\circ}:=V_{\cup-[r_{\circ}]}
for all
i\in V(R)
and index sets
I\subseteq[r]_{0}
.
Note that the packing instance
\mathscr{P}
naturally corresponds to a blow-up instance

(\mathcal{H},G,R,\{X^{H}_{i}\}_{i\in{V(R)},H\in\mathcal{H}},\{V_{i}\}_{i\in{V(R)}})

of size
(n,k,r+r_{\circ}+1)
. In particular, we also use the notation of Section
4
.
For the sake of a better readability, we stick to some conventions:

We will often use the letters
(Z,\mathcal{Z})\in\{(A,\mathcal{A}),(B,\mathcal{B})\}
as many arguments for candidacy graphs
\mathcal{A}_{I}
with respect to
G_{A}
, and candidacy graphs
\mathcal{B}_{i}
with respect to
G_{B}
are the same.
Whenever we write
xv\in E(\mathcal{Z}_{i})
for some
i\in[r]
, we tacitly assume that
x\in\mathscr{X}_{i},v\in V_{i}
. We usually denote edges in
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
by (non-calligraphic) letters
e,f
, and hyperedges in
\mathcal{A}_{I}
by
\mathscrs{a}
and a collection of edges from
\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})
by
\mathscrs{b}=\{b_{j}\}_{j\in J}
, where we allow to slightly abuse the notation and often treat
\mathscrs{b}
as
b_{\cup J}
.
Whenever we write
\{v_{i_{1}},\ldots,v_{i_{k}}\}\in E(G_{Z})
, we tacitly assume that
v_{i_{\ell}}\in V_{i_{\ell}}
for all
\ell\in[k]
; analogously for
\{x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{k}}\}\in E(H)
.
We usually refer to hyperedges in
G_{Z}
with letters
\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}
, hyperedges in
\mathcal{H}
with letters
\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f}
, and hyperedges in
R
with
\mathscrs{r}
.

The aim of this section is to map almost all vertices of
\mathscr{X}_{0}
into
V_{0}
by defining a function
\sigma\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\to V_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}

(that is,
x\sigma(x)\in E(\mathcal{A}_{0})
)
where
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\subseteq\mathscr{X}_{0}
, while maintaining several properties for the other candidacy graphs.
We identify such a function
\sigma
with its
corresponding edge set

M(\sigma)
defined as

(5.1)

\displaystyle M(\sigma):=\{xv\colon x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma},v\in V_{0},\sigma(x)=v\}.

To incorporate that
\sigma
has to be chosen such that each edge in
G_{A}
is used at most once, we define an edge set labelling
\psi
with respect to
\mathscr{P}
on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
as follows.
For every edge
xv\in E(\mathcal{A}_{0})
, we set

(5.2)

\displaystyle\psi(xv):=\left\{\phi_{\circ}^{-}(\mathscrs{e}\setminus\{x\})\cup\{v\}\colon x\in\mathscrs{e}\in E(\mathcal{H})\text{ with }\mathscrs{e}\setminus\{x\}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\circ}^{-}\right\}.

We defined the candidacy graphs
\mathcal{A}_{0}
in Definition
4.1
such that
xv\in E(\mathcal{A}_{0})
only if
\phi_{\circ}^{-}(\mathscrs{e}\setminus\{x\})\cup\{v\}\in E(G_{A})
for each such edge
\mathscrs{e}
as in (
5.2
).
That is,
\psi(xv)
encodes the set of edges in
G_{A}
that are used for the packing when mapping
x
onto
v
.
We say

(5.3)

\sigma\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\to V_{0}
is a
conflict-free packing
if
\sigma|_{\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\cap X_{0}^{H}}
is injective for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
\psi(e)\cap\psi(f)=\emptyset
for all distinct
e,f\in M(\sigma)
.

Crucially note that the property that
\psi(e)\cap\psi(f)=\emptyset
for all distinct
e,f\in M(\sigma)
will guarantee that every edge in
G_{A}
is used at most once.
For an illustration, see Figure
2
.

H_{1}
X_{-i_{1}}^{H_{1}}
X_{-i_{2}}^{H_{1}}
X_{0}^{H_{1}}
G_{A}
V_{-i_{1}}
V_{-i_{2}}
V_{0}
H_{2}
X_{-i_{1}}^{H_{2}}
X_{-i_{2}}^{H_{2}}
X_{0}^{H_{2}}
\phi_{\circ}^{-}
\phi_{\circ}^{-}
v_{0}
x_{1}
x_{2}
\mathscrs{e}_{1}
\mathscrs{e}_{2}
\mathcal{A}_{0}
\mathscrs{g}

Figure 2
.
We have
\mathcal{H}=\{H_{1},H_{2}\}
and
k=3
.
If
\phi_{\circ}^{-}(\mathscrs{e}_{1}\setminus\{x_{1}\})=\phi_{\circ}^{-}(\mathscrs{e}_{2}\setminus\{x_{2}\})=\mathscrs{g}\setminus\{v_{0}\}
, then we add the label
\mathscrs{g}
to the edge set label
\psi(x_{1}v_{0})
and
\psi(x_{2}v_{0})
of the edges
x_{1}v_{0}
and
x_{2}v_{0}
of the candidacy graph
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
This captures the information that if
x_{1}
or
x_{2}
are mapped onto
v_{0}
by
\sigma
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
, then this embedding uses the edge
\mathscrs{g}\in E(G_{A})
.

Given a conflict-free packing
\sigma\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\to V_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
, we update the remaining candidacy graphs with respect to
\sigma
.
To account for the vertices in
\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
that are left unembedded by
\sigma
, we will consider an extension
\sigma^{+}
of
\sigma
such that
\sigma^{+}
also maps every vertex
x_{0}\in\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
to
V_{0}
and
\sigma^{+}|_{X_{0}^{H}}
is injective for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
(and hence bijective).
We call such a
\sigma^{+}
a
cluster-injective extension
of
\sigma
.
The purpose of
\sigma^{+}
is that
\sigma^{+}|_{\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}}
will serve as a ‘dummy’ extension resulting in an easier analysis of the packing process as
\sigma^{+}
will impose further restriction that culminate in more consistent candidacy graphs.
Using Definition
4.1
, we will consider the
(updated) candidacy graphs

A_{I}^{H}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
with respect to
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
and
G_{A}
for index sets
I\subseteq[r]
, as well as the
(updated) candidacy graphs

B_{j}^{H}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
with respect to
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
and
G_{B}
for
j\in V(R)
.

To track our packing process, we carefully maintain quasirandom properties of the candidacy graphs throughout the entire procedure.
Our Approximate Packing Lemma will guarantee that we can find a conflict-free packing that behaves like an idealized random packing with respect to given sets of
edge testers
(as defined in Definition
4.5
), and with respect to weight functions
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}^{=}\to[0,s]
for
\ell\leq s
that we will call
local testers
.

To that end, assume we are given a packing instance
\mathscr{P}=(\mathcal{H},G_{A},G_{B},R,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\phi_{\circ}^{-},\phi_{\circ})
of size
(n,k,r,r_{\circ})
, and
\mathbf{d}=(d_{A},d_{B},(d_{i,A})_{i\in[r]_{0}},(d_{i,B})_{i\in{V(R)}})
,
and
q,t\in\mathbb{N}
,
as well as a set
\mathcal{W}_{edge}
of edge testers.
We say
\mathscr{P}
is an
({\varepsilon},q,t,\mathbf{d})
-packing instance with suitable edge testers
\mathcal{W}_{edge}

if
|X_{i}^{H}|=|V_{i}|=(1\pm{\varepsilon})n
for all
i\in{V(R)}
, and the following properties are satisfied
(recall (
4.2
) and Definitions
4.5
–
4.7
for
(P2)
–
(P5)
, respectively, and that we write
J_{XV}
for
J_{X}\cup J_{V}
):

(P1)

for all
i\in{V(R)}
and all pairs of disjoint sets
\mathcal{S}_{A},\mathcal{S}_{B}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)\colon i\in\mathscrs{r}}V_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}\setminus\{i\}}
with
|\mathcal{S}_{A}\cup\mathcal{S}_{B}|\leq t
, we have

\big|V_{i}\cap N_{G_{A}}(\mathcal{S}_{A})\cap N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S}_{B})\big|=(1\pm{\varepsilon})d_{A}^{|\mathcal{S}_{A}|}d_{B}^{|\mathcal{S}_{B}|}n;

(P2)

for all
H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[r]_{0},j\in{V(R)}
, we have that
A^{H}_{i}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{i,A})
-super-regular and
({\varepsilon},q)
-well-intersecting with respect to
G_{A}
, and
B^{H}_{j}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{j,B})
-super-regular and
({\varepsilon},q)
-well-intersecting with respect to
G_{B}
;

(P3)

for every edge tester
(\omega,\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})\in\mathcal{W}_{edge}
with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
,
(\phi_{\circ}^{-},\phi_{\circ})
,
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, with centres
\mathscrs{c}\in V_{\sqcup I}
for
I\subseteq{V(R)}
,
I_{r_{0}}:=(I\cap[r]_{0})\setminus J
,
and patterns
\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})\in(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{r_{\circ}+r+1})^{4}
,
we have that

\displaystyle\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}\Big)\prod_{Z\in\{A,B\}}d_{Z}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{Z}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{Z,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}])\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}};

(P4)

for all
\mathscrs{g}\in E(G_{A}[V_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}])
for some
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
with
\mathscrs{r}\cap[r]_{0}\neq\emptyset
, and all
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{r_{\circ}+r+1}
, the set
\mathcal{W}_{edge}
contains the edge tester for
\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})
;

(P5)

for all
\mathscrs{g}=\{v_{i_{1}},\ldots,v_{i_{k}}\},\mathscrs{h}=\{w_{j_{1}},\ldots,w_{j_{k}}\}\in E(G_{A})
with
v_{i_{k}}=w_{j_{k}}
and
I:=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}\neq\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\}=:J
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})\right|\leq\max\left\{n^{k-\left|(I\cup J)\cap-[r_{\circ}]\right|+{\varepsilon}},n^{\varepsilon}\right\}.

Note that
(P1)
also implies that
G_{Z}
is
(3{\varepsilon},t,d_{Z})
-typical with respect to
R
for each
Z\in\{A,B\}
.

Furthermore, we call a function
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}^{=}\to[0,s]
for
\ell\leq s
a
local
s
-tester (for the
({\varepsilon},q,t,\mathbf{d})
-packing instance
\mathscr{P}
)
if
\|\omega\|_{\ell^{\prime}}\leq n^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}}
for every
\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell]
.
We introduce some more notation:

(5.4)

Let
E_{i}^{R}:=\{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)\colon\{0,i\}=\mathscrs{r}\cap([r]_{0}\cup\{i\})\}
and
b_{i}:=|E_{i}^{R}|=\dg_{R[-[r_{\circ}]\cup\{0,i\}]}(\{0,i\})
for each
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
.
For
I\subseteq{V(R)}
, let
b_{I}:=\sum_{i\in I\setminus\{0\}}b_{i}
.
For
i\in[r],j\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
, let
d_{i,A}^{new}:=d_{i,A}d_{A}^{b_{i}}
and
d_{j,B}^{new}:=d_{j,B}d_{B}^{b_{j}}
.

Note that for
i\in{V(R)}\setminus N_{R_{\ast}}[0]
, we have
b_{i}=0
and thus
d_{i,Z}^{new}=d_{i,Z}
for
Z\in\{A,B\}
.

5.2.
Approximate Packing Lemma

We now state the Approximate Packing Lemma, which is the key tool for the proof of our main result.

Lemma 5.1

(Approximate Packing Lemma)
.

Let
1/n\ll{\varepsilon}\ll{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\ll 1/t\ll 1/k,1/q,1/r,1/(r_{\circ}+1),1/s.

Suppose
(\mathcal{H},G_{A},G_{B},R,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\phi_{\circ}^{-},\phi_{\circ})
is an
({\varepsilon},q,t,\mathbf{d})
-packing instance of size
(n,k,r,r_{\circ})
with
\mathbf{d}\geq n^{-{\varepsilon}}
and suitable
s
-edge-testers
\mathcal{W}_{edge}
. Suppose further that
\mathcal{W}_{local}
is a set of local
s
-testers,
\mathcal{W}_{0}
is a set of tuples
(\omega,c)
with
\omega\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}\to[0,s]
,
c\in V_{0}
, and
|\mathcal{W}_{edge}|,

|\mathcal{W}_{local}|,|\mathcal{W}_{0}|\leq n^{4\log n}
,
|\mathcal{H}|\leq n^{2k}
, as well as

e_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}})\leq d_{A}n^{k}
for all
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
.

Then there is a conflict-free packing
\sigma\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\to V_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
and a cluster-injective extension
\sigma^{+}
of
\sigma
such that for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
, we have
|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\cap X_{0}^{H}|\geq(1-{\varepsilon}^{\prime})n
, and
for all index sets
I_{A}\subseteq[r]
,
I_{B}\in{V(R)}
and
(Z,\mathcal{Z})\in\{(A,\mathcal{A}),(B,\mathcal{B})\}
, there exist spanning subgraphs
Z_{I_{Z}}^{H,new}
of
the candidacy graphs
Z^{H}_{I_{Z}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
with respect to
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
and
G_{Z}

(where
\mathcal{Z}_{I_{Z}}^{new}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}Z_{I_{Z}}^{H,new}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{new}
is the collection of all
\mathcal{Z}_{I_{Z}}^{new}
) such that

(I)
L
5.1

Z^{H,new}_{i}
is
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},d_{i,Z}^{new})
-super-regular and
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},q+\Delta(R))
-well-intersecting with respect to
G_{Z}
for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
, and all
i\in[r]
if
Z=A
, and all
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
if
Z=B
;

(II)
L
5.1

for every (general)
s
-edge tester
(\omega,\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})\in\mathcal{W}_{edge}
with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
,
(\phi_{\circ}^{-},\phi_{\circ})
,
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, with centres
\mathscrs{c}\in V_{\sqcup I}
for
I\subseteq{V(R)}
,
I_{r}:=(I\cap[r])\setminus J
,
I_{r}\cup J\neq\emptyset
, and patterns
\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})\in(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{r_{\circ}+r+1})^{4}
,
the
s
-edge tester
(\omega^{new},\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
defined as in Definition
4.5
with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
,
(\phi_{\circ}^{-}\cup\sigma,\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
,
\mathcal{A}^{new}
and
\mathcal{B}^{new}
satisfies

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0}=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2}\right)\prod_{Z\in\{A,B\}}d_{Z}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{Z}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{Z,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0})\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}};

(III)
L
5.1

\omega(M(\sigma))=(1\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2})\omega(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))/(d_{0,A}n)^{\ell}\pm n^{\varepsilon}
for every local
s
-tester
\omega\in\mathcal{W}_{local}
with
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}^{=}\to[0,s]
;

(IV)
L
5.1

\omega\left(\{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\colon\sigma^{+}(x)=c\}\right)\leq\omega(\mathscr{X}_{0})/n^{1-{\varepsilon}}+n^{\varepsilon}

for every
(\omega,c)\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
.

Properties
(I)L
and
(II)L
ensure that
(P2)
and
(P3)
are also satisfied for the updated candidacy graphs
\mathcal{A}^{new}
and
\mathcal{B}^{new}
, respectively.
Property
(III)L
states that
\sigma
behaves like a random packing with respect to the local testers, which for instance can be used to establish
(P5)
for future packing rounds.
Property
(IV)L
allows to control the weight on vertices that are not embedded by
\sigma
but are nevertheless mapped onto a specific vertex
c
by the extension
\sigma^{+}
.

Proof.

We split the proof into three parts.
In Part
A
we construct an auxiliary supergraph
H_{+}
of every
H\in\mathcal{H}
by adding some hyperedges to
H[X^{H}_{\cup{\mathscrs{r}}}]
for every
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
in order to make the packing procedure more uniform.
In Part
B
we construct an auxiliary hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
for
\mathcal{A}_{0}
such that we can use Theorem
3.8
to find a conflict-free packing in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
In order to be able to apply Theorem
3.8
, we exploit
(P5)
as well as
(P2)
together with
(P3)
to control
\Delta_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{aux})
and
\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{aux})
, respectively.
In Part
C
we define weight functions and employ the conclusion of Theorem
3.8
to establish
(I)L
–
(III)L
.

Let
\Delta_{R}:=2\binom{r+r_{\circ}}{k-1}
. Note that
\Delta(R),\Delta(\mathcal{H}),b_{i}\leq\Delta_{R}
for all
i\in[r]
. For simplicity we write
d_{0}:=d_{0,A}
and
{\hat{\varepsilon}}:={\varepsilon}^{1/2}
.
Further, we choose a new constant
\Delta
such that
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\ll 1/\Delta\ll 1/t
.

Part A
.

Construction of
H_{+}

We construct an auxiliary supergraph
H_{+}
of
H
by artificially adding some edges to
H
for every
\mathscrs{r}\in E_{i}^{R}
and
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
.
(Recall (
5.4
) for the definition of
E_{i}^{R}
and note that it can be that
\mathscrs{r}\in E_{i}^{R}\cap E_{j}^{R}
for
i,j\in-[r_{\circ}]
.)
For every
H\in\mathcal{H}
, we proceed as follows.
We obtain
H_{+}
from
H
by adding a minimal number of hyperedges of size
k
subject to the conditions that for all
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
and
\mathscrs{r}\in E_{i}^{R}
, an
H_{+}
-edge
meets every cluster in
H[X^{H}_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}]
exactly once, and

(a)

\dg_{H_{+}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]}(x_{i})\in\{1,2\}

for all
x_{i}\in X_{i}^{H}
, and
\dg_{H_{+}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]}(x_{0})\leq 2

for all
x_{0}\in X_{0}^{H}
;

(b)

for all
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H_{+}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}])
, we have
|\{x\in\mathscrs{e}\colon\dg_{H_{+}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]}(x)=2\}|\leq 1
;

(c)

for all
\{x_{0},x_{i}\}\in X_{0}^{H}\sqcup X_{i}^{H}
, if

\{x_{0},x_{i}\}\subseteq\mathscrs{e}
for some
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H_{+})\setminus E(H)
, then
\{x_{0},x_{i}\}\nsubseteq\mathscrs{f}
for all
\mathscrs{f}\in E(H)
.

Note that
(a)
–
(c)
can be met because
|X_{i}^{H}|=(1\pm{\varepsilon})n
for all
i\in{V(R)}
and
\Delta(R)\leq\Delta_{R}
.
For all
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
, let
H_{+}^{i}
be an arbitrary but fixed
k
-graph
H\subseteq H_{+}^{i}\subseteq H_{+}
such that for all
\mathscrs{r}\in E_{i}^{R}
, we have
\dg_{H_{+}^{i}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]}(x_{i})=1
.
Observe that by the construction of
H_{+}
, we have
\dg_{H_{+}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]}(x_{i})=1
for all
x_{i}\in X_{i}^{H}
,
i\in[r]
,
\mathscrs{r}\in E_{i}^{R}
, and thus
H_{+}^{i}=H_{+}
for all
i\in[r]
.
We make some observations.

(5.5)

For all
x\in X_{i}^{H},i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
, we have
\sum_{\mathscrs{r}\in E_{i}^{R}}\dg_{H_{+}^{i}[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]}(x)=b_{i}
.

(5.6)

By
(a)
, for every
x\in X_{i}^{H},i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
, there are at most
\Delta_{R}
vertices
x^{\prime}\in X_{i}^{H}\setminus\{x\}
such that
x
and
x^{\prime}
have a common neighbour in
X_{0}^{H}
in
H_{+}[\mathcal{X}_{\circ},X_{0}^{H},X_{i}^{H}]
, that is,
e_{x}\cap e_{x^{\prime}}\cap X_{0}^{H}\neq\emptyset
with
e_{y}\in E(H_{+}[\mathcal{X}_{\circ},X_{0}^{H},X_{i}^{H}])
and
y\in e_{y}
for both
y\in\{x,x^{\prime}\}
.

(5.7)

If
\{x_{0},x_{i}\}
lies in an edge of
H_{+}-H
, then
\{x_{0},x_{i}\}
does not lie in an edge of
H
.

(5.8)

If
|\mathscrs{e}\cap X^{H}_{\cup{[r]}}|\geq 2
, then
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H)
for all
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H_{+})
.

We introduce some simpler notation how to denote edges in
H_{+}
(respectively
H_{+}^{i}
) that contain a vertex
x\in X_{0}^{H}
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{+}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}H_{+}
, and for
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
, let
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{i}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}H_{+}^{i}
.
For all
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}
and
\mathscrs{y}\in\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I}
for some
I\subseteq{V(R)}
, let

(5.9)

\displaystyle E_{x,\mathscrs{y}}:=\big\{\mathscrs{e}\in E(\mathcal{H}_{+}[\mathcal{X}_{\circ},\mathscr{X}_{0},\mathscr{X}_{\cup I}])\colon

\displaystyle x\in\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{e}\cap\mathscr{X}_{\cup{I}}\subseteq\{x\}\cup\mathscrs{y}\cup\mathcal{X}_{\circ},\mathscrs{e}\cap(\mathscrs{y}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0})\neq\emptyset,

\displaystyle\text{if $\mathscrs{e}\cap(\mathscrs{y}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0})\in\mathscr{X}_{i}$ for some $i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}$, then $\mathscrs{e}\in E(\mathcal{H}_{+}^{i})$}\big\}.

That is,
E_{x,\mathscrs{y}}
contains essentially all
\mathcal{H}_{+}
-edges that contain
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}
and a non-empty subset of
\mathscrs{y}
and whose remaining vertices are already embedded and lie in
\mathcal{X}_{\circ}
.
In particular, if
\mathscrs{y}=y
is a single vertex
y\in\mathscr{X}_{i}
, then
E_{x,y}
contains all
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{i}
-edges that contain
x
and
y
and whose remaining vertices lie in
\mathcal{X}_{\circ}
.
Hence, note that by definition of
\mathcal{H}_{+}
and as observed in (
5.5
), we have that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\big|\textstyle\bigcup_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}}E_{x,y}\big|=b_{i}\text{ for all $y\in\mathscr{X}_{i},i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}$.}

Part B
.

Applying Theorem
3.8

Our strategy is to utilize Theorem
3.8
to find the required conflict-free packing
\sigma
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
To that end, we will define an auxiliary hypergraph
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
for
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
Let
\psi\colon E(\mathcal{A}_{0})\to 2^{\mathcal{E}}
be the edge set labelling with respect to the packing instance as defined in (
5.2
).
For all
H\in\mathcal{H}
, the hypergraph
H[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]
is a matching if
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
and empty otherwise, and thus we have that
\|\psi\|\leq\binom{r_{\circ}}{k-1}\leq\Delta_{R}
.
In the following, we may assume that
|\psi(e)|=\Delta_{R}
for all
e\in E(\mathcal{A}_{0})
as we may simply add distinct artificial dummy labels that we ignore afterwards again.

Further, let
(V_{0}^{H})_{H\in\mathcal{H}}
be disjoint copies of
V_{0}
, and for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
e=x_{0}v_{0}\in E(A_{0}^{H})
, let
e^{H}:=x_{0}v_{0}^{H}
where
v_{0}^{H}
is the copy of
v_{0}
in
V_{0}^{H}
.
Let
\mathscrs{h}_{e}:=e^{H}\cup\psi(e)
for each
e\in E(A_{0}^{H})
,
H\in\mathcal{H}
and let
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
be the
(\Delta_{R}+2)
-graph with vertex set
\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0}^{H})\cup\mathcal{E}

and edge set
\{\mathscrs{h}_{e}\colon e\in E(\mathcal{A}_{0})\}
.
A key property of the construction of
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
is a bijection between conflict-free packings
\sigma
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
and matchings
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
by assigning
\sigma
to
\mathcal{M}=\{\mathscrs{h}_{e}\colon e\in M(\sigma)\}
. (Recall that
M(\sigma)
is the edge set corresponding to
\sigma
as defined in (
5.1
).)

Step 1.

Estimating
\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{aux})
and
\Delta_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{aux})

In order to apply Theorem
3.8
to
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
, we estimate
\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{aux})
and
\Delta_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{aux})
.
We first claim that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\Delta(\mathcal{H}_{aux})\leq(1+{\varepsilon}^{2/3})d_{0}n=:\Delta_{aux}.

Since
A_{0}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{0})
-super-regular and
|X_{0}^{H}|=|V_{0}|=(1\pm{\varepsilon})n
for each
H\in\mathcal{H}
, we have that an appropriate upper bound on
\Delta_{\psi}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
immediately establishes (
5.11
).
In the following
we derive such an upper bound on
\Delta_{\psi}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
by employing property
(P3)
.
For all
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
with
0\in\mathscrs{r}
,
|\mathscrs{r}\cap-[r_{\circ}]|=k-1
, and all
\mathscrs{g}\in E(G_{A}[V_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}])
with
\mathscrs{g}\setminus\mathcal{V}_{\circ}=\{v_{0}\}
, note that
\bigcup_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}}\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})
contains by Definition
4.6
all vertices
x_{0}\in N_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}(v_{0})
(compare with (
4.8
)) that are contained in an
\mathcal{H}
-edge that could be mapped onto
\mathscrs{g}
with respect to
\phi_{\circ}^{-}
and
\mathcal{A}
(compare with (
4.9
)).
Hence, by the definition of the edge set labelling
\psi
in (
5.2
),

\mathscrs{g}
appears as a label of
\psi
on at most

\sum_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}}|\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})|

edges of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
Note that by Definition
4.3
of
E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\mathscrs{r})
, we obtain

(5.12)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}}|E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\mathscrs{r})|=e_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}})\leq d_{A}n^{k},

where the last inequality holds by assumption of Lemma
5.1
.
For all
x_{0}\in N_{A_{0}^{H}}(v_{0})
for some
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
let
\mathscrs{g}^{-1}_{x_{0}}:=(\phi_{\circ}^{-}|_{V(H)})^{-1}(\mathscrs{g}\setminus\{v_{0}\})\cup\{x_{0}\}
.
If
\mathscrs{g}^{-1}_{x_{0}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})
for
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{r}
, then we have by (
4.9
) that
\mathscrs{g}^{-1}_{x_{0}}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\mathscrs{r})
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}^{A}\left(\mathscrs{g}^{-1}_{x_{0}},\emptyset\right)=\mathbf{p},\quad\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}\left(\mathscrs{g}^{-1}_{x_{0}},\emptyset\right)=\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \|\mathbf{p}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{2nd}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:norms equal}}}{{=}}-1.

By
(P4)
, the set
\mathcal{W}_{edge}
contains the (simple) edge tester
(\omega,\omega_{\iota},\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd})
for
\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})
(as defined in Definition
4.6
) with
\omega(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))=|\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})|

and
\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}})=|E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\mathscrs{r})|
.
Hence, by
(P3)
(with
I=\mathscrs{r}
,
J=J_{X}=J_{V}=\emptyset
,
\mathbf{p}^{A}=\mathbf{p}
,
\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}=\mathbf{p}^{2nd}
,
\mathbf{p}^{B}=\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}=\mathbf{0}
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{\psi}(\mathcal{A}_{0})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{{\eqref{eq:sum E(p,p,r)}}}}}{{\leq}}\sum_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}}|\mathcal{X}_{\mathscrs{g},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{item:P edge tester}\penalty\ }}{{\leq}}\sum_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd}}\Big((1+{\varepsilon})d_{A}^{-1}d_{0}\frac{|E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{p}^{2nd},\mathscrs{r})|}{n^{k-1}}+n^{{\varepsilon}}\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:sum E(p,p,r)}}}{{\leq}}(1+{\varepsilon}^{2/3})d_{0}n.

This establishes (
5.11
).

Next, we claim that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}(\mathcal{H}_{aux})\leq n^{\varepsilon}\leq\Delta_{aux}^{1-{\varepsilon}^{2}}.

Note that the codegree in
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
of two vertices in
\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0}^{H})
is at most 1, and similarly, the codegree in
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
of a vertex in
\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0}^{H})
and a label in
\mathcal{E}
is at most 1 because
\Delta_{\psi}(A_{0}^{H})\leq 1
for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
.
Hence, an appropriate upper bound on
\Delta_{\psi}^{c}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
establishes (
5.13
).
In the following we derive such an upper bound on
\Delta_{\psi}^{c}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
by employing
(P5)
.
For all
\mathscrs{g}=\{v_{i_{1}},\ldots,v_{i_{k}}\},\mathscrs{h}=\{w_{j_{1}},\ldots,w_{j_{k}}\}\in E(G_{A})
with
v_{i_{k}}=w_{j_{k}}\in V_{0}
,
(\mathscrs{g}\cup\mathscrs{h})\setminus\{v_{i_{k}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\circ}
, and
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}\neq\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\}
, note that
\mathscrs{g}
and
\mathscrs{h}
appear together as labels of
\psi
on at most
|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})|
edges of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
This follows immediately from Definition
4.7
of
E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})
.
Note further that
\left|\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k},j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\}\cap-[r_{\circ}]\right|\geq k
because
(\mathscrs{g}\cup\mathscrs{h})\setminus\{v_{i_{k}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{\circ}
, and
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}\neq\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\}
.
Hence, by
(P5)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{\circ}^{-}}(\mathcal{A})\right|\leq\max\left\{n^{k-\left|\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k},j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\}\cap-[r_{\circ}]\right|+{\varepsilon}},n^{\varepsilon}\right\}=n^{\varepsilon},

and thus,
\Delta_{\psi}^{c}(\mathcal{A}_{0})\leq n^{\varepsilon}
, which establishes (
5.13
).

Step 2.

Applying Theorem
3.8
to
\mathcal{H}_{aux}

Suppose
\mathcal{W}=\bigcup_{\ell\in[\Delta]}\mathcal{W}_{\ell}
is a set of size at most
n^{4\log n}
of given weight functions
\omega\in\mathcal{W}_{\ell}
for
\ell\in[\Delta]
with
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}\to[0,\Delta]
and

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\ell^{\prime}}\leq n^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}}\text{ for every $\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell]$.}

Note that every weight function
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}\to[0,\Delta]
naturally corresponds to a weight function
\omega_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}}\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{H}_{aux})}{\ell}\to[0,\Delta]
by defining
\omega_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}}(\{\mathscrs{h}_{e_{1}},\ldots,\mathscrs{h}_{e_{\ell}}\}):=\omega(\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell}\})
.
We will explicitly specify
\mathcal{W}
in Part
C
, where every weight function
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}\to[0,\Delta]
in
\mathcal{W}_{\ell}
for
\ell\in[\Delta]
will be defined such that
{\rm supp}(\omega)\subseteq\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{\ell}^{=}
and in particular, such that the corresponding weight function
\omega_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}}
will also be clean,
that is
{\rm supp}(\omega_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}})\subseteq\binom{E(\mathcal{H}_{aux})}{\ell}^{=}
.
Our main idea is to find a hypergraph matching in
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
that behaves like a typical random matching with respect to
\{\omega_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}}\colon\omega\in\mathcal{W}\}
in order to establish
(I)L
–
(III)L
.

Suppose
\ell\in[\Delta]
and
\omega\in\mathcal{W}_{\ell}
.
If
\omega(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))\geq n^{\ell+{\varepsilon}/2}
, define
\tilde{\omega}:=\omega
.
Otherwise, arbitrarily choose
\tilde{\omega}\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}\to[0,\Delta]
such that
\omega\leq\tilde{\omega}
,
\tilde{\omega}
satisfies that
{\rm supp}(\tilde{\omega})\subseteq\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{\ell}^{=}
and in particular
{\rm supp}(\tilde{\omega}_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}})\subseteq\binom{E(\mathcal{H}_{aux})}{\ell}^{=}
,
\tilde{\omega}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))=n^{\ell+{\varepsilon}/2}
, and
\|\tilde{\omega}\|_{\ell^{\prime}}\leq n^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}+2{\varepsilon}^{2}}
for all
\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell]
.
By (
5.11
) and (
5.13
), we can apply Theorem
3.8

(with
\Delta_{aux},{\varepsilon}^{2},\Delta_{R}+2,\Delta,\{\tilde{\omega}_{\mathcal{H}_{aux}}\colon{\omega}\in\mathcal{W}_{\ell}\}
playing the roles of the parameters
\Delta,\delta,r,L,\mathcal{W}_{\ell}
of Theorem
3.8
, respectively) to obtain a matching
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathcal{H}_{aux}
that corresponds to a conflict-free packing
\sigma\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\to V_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
with its corresponding edge set
M(\sigma)
that satisfies the following properties (where
{\hat{\varepsilon}}={\varepsilon}^{1/2}
):

(5.15)

\displaystyle\omega(M(\sigma))

\displaystyle=(1\pm{\varepsilon})(1-\ell{\varepsilon}^{2/3})\frac{\omega(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))}{(d_{0}n)^{\ell}}\pm n^{\varepsilon}

(5.16)

\displaystyle=(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{\omega(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))}{(d_{0}n)^{\ell}}\pm n^{\varepsilon}\text{ for all }\omega\in\mathcal{W}_{\ell},\ell\in[\Delta].

Part C
.

Employing weight functions to conclude
(I)L
–
(III)L

Let
\sigma\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\to V_{0}
be the conflict-free packing in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
as obtained in Part
B

and let
\sigma^{+}
be a cluster-injective extension of
\sigma
chosen uniformly and independently at random.
We will show that the random
\sigma^{+}
satisfies with high probability the conclusions of the lemma and thus, there exists a suitable cluster-injective extension
\sigma^{+}
by picking one such extension deterministically.
We may assume that (
5.16
) holds for a set of weight functions
\mathcal{W}
.
Each of these weight functions will only depend on our input parameters.
Hence, we could define them right away but for the sake of a cleaner presentation we postpone their definitions to the specific situations when we employ those weight functions to establish
(I)L
–
(III)L
.
We now define the candidacy graphs
Z_{I_{Z}}^{H,new}\subseteq Z_{I_{Z}}^{H}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
for
Z\in\{A,B\}
and all index sets
I=I_{A}\subseteq[r]
, and
i=I_{B}\in{V(R)}
.
If
I_{Z}\cap\mathscrs{r}=\emptyset
for all
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
with
0\in\mathscrs{r}
, then we set
Z_{I_{Z}}^{H,new}:=Z_{I_{Z}}^{H}
.
Otherwise, let

(5.17)

\displaystyle A_{I}^{H,new}:=A_{I}^{H_{+}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}),\quad\text{ and }\quad B_{i}^{H,new}:=B_{i}^{H_{+}^{i}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}),

with
A_{I}^{H_{+}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
defined as in Definition
4.1
with respect to
H_{+}
,
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
and
G_{A}
, as well as
B_{i}^{H_{+}^{i}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
defined with respect to
H_{+}^{i}
,
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
and
G_{B}
.

Before we establish
(I)L
–
(III)L
, we first estimate
|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\cap X_{0}^{H}|
for each
H\in\mathcal{H}
.
We define a weight function
\omega_{H}\colon E(A_{0}^{H})\to\{0,1\}
for each
H\in\mathcal{H}
by
\omega_{H}(e):=\mathbbm{1}{\{e\in E(A_{0}^{H})\}}
, and add
\omega_{H}
to
\mathcal{W}
.
Note that
\omega_{H}(E(A_{0}^{H}))=(1\pm 3{\varepsilon})d_{0}n^{2}
because
A_{0}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{0})
-super-regular by
(P2)
.
By (
5.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\cap X_{0}^{H}|=\omega_{H}(M(\sigma))

\displaystyle=(1\pm 5{\varepsilon})(1-{\varepsilon}^{2/3})\frac{d_{0}n^{2}}{d_{0}n}\pm n^{\varepsilon},

and thus,

(5.18)

\displaystyle(1-{\varepsilon}^{2/3}/2)n\geq|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\cap X_{0}^{H}|\geq(1-{\hat{\varepsilon}})n.

We first prove
(II)L
, as we can use this for establishing
(I)L
.

Step 3.

Preparation for checking
(II)L

We will even show that
(II)L
holds for edge testers in
\mathcal{W}_{edge}\cup\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{\prime}
is a set of suitable edge testers satisfying
(P3)
that we will explicitly specify in Step
5.2
when establishing
(I)L
.
Throughout Steps
5.2
–
5.2
let
(\omega,\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})\in\mathcal{W}_{edge}\cup\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{\prime}
be fixed.
That is, we fix an index set
I\subseteq{V(R)}
,
J\subseteq I
, disjoint sets
J_{X},J_{V}\subseteq J
, and let
I_{r_{0}}:=(I\cap[r]_{0})\setminus J
,
I_{r}:=(I\cap[r])\setminus J
,
J_{XV}:=J_{X}\cup J_{V}
, and we fix

\omega\colon E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\to[0,s]
,
\omega_{\iota}\colon\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I}\to[0,s]
,
\mathscrs{c}=\{c_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in V_{\sqcup I}
, and
\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})\in(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{r_{\circ}+r+1})^{4}
with
\mathbf{p}^{Z}=(p_{i}^{Z})_{i\in{V(R)}},\mathbf{p}^{Z,2nd}=(p_{i}^{Z,2nd})_{i\in{V(R)}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{r_{\circ}+r+1}
for
Z\in\{A,B\}
, and we may assume that
I\cap\mathscrs{r}\neq\emptyset
for some
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
with
0\in\mathscrs{r}
because otherwise we do not update the weight of the edge tester.
Recall that the statement
(II)L
concerns the weight of the edge tester
(\omega^{new},\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
defined as in Definition
4.5
with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
,
(\phi_{\circ}^{-}\cup\sigma,\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
,
\mathcal{A}^{new}
and
\mathcal{B}^{new}
.

We will consider three different cases depending on whether
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
,
0\in I\setminus J
, and
0\in J_{XV}
.
Even though we proceed similarly in each of these cases, the effects on
(II)L
are quite different in each scenario as we try to illude in the following.
Recall that
(II)L
ensures that
(P3)
is also satisfied for the updated candidacy graphs.
If
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
,
we have to update the density factors whereas the magnitude of
{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}/{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}])\setminus J|}}
in
(P3)
equals the magnitude of
{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}/{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0})\setminus J|}}
in
(II)L
.
In contrast, if
0\in I\setminus J
, we additionally have to ensure that the magnitude of
{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}/{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}])\setminus J|}}
in
(P3)
will be updated by a factor of
n^{-1}
to obtain the the magnitude of
{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}/{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0})\setminus J|}}
in
(II)L
.
If
0\in J_{XV}
the magnitudes are again equal, but besides updating the densities we additionally have to consider that
0\in J_{XV}
and thus
(P3)
will potentially be updated by the factor

(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0}=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2})=0\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2}
.

We collect some common notation that will be used to establish
(II)L
.
Recall that the centres
\mathscrs{c}
are fixed and for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}=\{\mathscrs{a},\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})
with
\mathscrs{b}=\{b_{j}\}_{j\in J}
, we have
\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0
only if
\{c_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r_{0}}\cup J}=(\mathscrs{a}\cup\mathscrs{b})\cap V_{\cup{(I_{r_{0}}\cup J)}}
by Definition
4.5
of an edge tester in (
4.7
).
(Recall that we allow to treat
\mathscrs{b}=\{b_{j}\}_{j\in J}\in\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})
as
b_{\cup J}
.)
To that end, for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
with
\{c_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r_{0}}\cup J}=(\mathscrs{a}\cup\mathscrs{b})\cap V_{\cup{(I_{r_{0}}\cup J)}}
, let
\mathscrs{y}_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}=\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r_{0}}\cup J}:=(\mathscrs{a}\cup\mathscrs{b})\cap X^{H}_{\cup{(I_{r_{0}}\cup J)}}
.
Our overall strategy in all three cases is to define for vertices
x
in some set
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\subseteq X_{0}^{H}
a target set
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
of suitable images for
x
such that if all

x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
are embedded into
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
, then
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
(or
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\{c_{0},y_{0}\}\}
if
0\in I\setminus J
) is an element in
E(A_{I_{r}}^{H,new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H,new})
.
Hence for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
x\in X_{0}^{H}
and
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
with
\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0
, we define the following sets.
We give more motivation for these definitions in the subsequent paragraph.
For an edge
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H)
, let
\mathscrs{r}{e}\in E(R)
be such that
\mathscrs{e}\in X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}{e}}^{H}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\phi_{\circ}(\mathscrs{e})\cup\{c_{i}\}_{i\in\mathscrs{r}{e}\cap I_{r}}\colon\mathscrs{e}\in E_{x,\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}}\big\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}

\displaystyle:=\big\{S=\phi_{\circ}(\mathscrs{e}\setminus\{y_{j}\})\cup\{c_{i}\}_{i\in\mathscrs{r}{e}\cap(I_{r}\cup\{j\})}\colon|S|=k-1,\mathscrs{e}\in E_{x,\mathscrs{y}_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}},j\in J\setminus\{0\},y_{j}\in\mathscrs{e}\big\};

\displaystyle V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}

\displaystyle:=V_{0}\cap N_{G_{A}}(\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}})\cap N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J});

\displaystyle T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}

\displaystyle:=V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\cap N_{A_{0}^{H}}(x).

That is,
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}
are sets of
(k-1)
-sets.
In general, these
(k-1)
-sets consist of the image
\phi_{\circ}(\mathscrs{e})=\phi_{\circ}(\mathscrs{e}\cap\mathcal{X}_{\circ})
of an
H_{+}
-edge
\mathscrs{e}\in E_{x,\mathscrs{y}_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
together with the centres corresponding to the clusters that
\mathscrs{e}
intersects.
The set
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}
contains all
(k-1)
-sets that only intersect with clusters of
I_{r}
, whereas
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}
contains
(k-1)
-sets that intersect with a cluster of
J\setminus\{0\}
.
Consequently,
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
is the intersection of the
A_{0}^{H}
-neighbourhood of
x
in
V_{0}
with the common neighbourhood
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
in
G_{A}
and
G_{B}
of all these
(k-1)
-sets in
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}
(see also Figures
3
and
4
).
Note that
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}\cup\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}=\emptyset
if
E_{x,\mathscrs{y}_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}=\emptyset
.
Further, since
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})>0
and it is required for the edge tester
\omega
that
\phi_{\circ}
does not map any vertices
\{y_{j}\}_{j\in J\cap-[r_{\circ}]}
onto its centres by
(v)D
, we have that
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}
are disjoint sets.
We estimate the sizes of
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
and
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
in Step
5.2
.
Further, for
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}=\{\mathscrs{a},\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})
, let
x{a}:=\mathscrs{a}\cap X_{0}^{H}
,
x{b}:=\mathscrs{b}\cap X_{0}^{H}
(note that
x{a}
or
x{b}
might be empty), and

(5.19)

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{x\in X_{0}^{H}\setminus\{x{a}\}\colon|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}\cup\mathcal{S}_{x,\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b},J}|\geq 1\right\}.

Step 3.1.

Weight functions to establish
(II)L

We emphasize again that the general strategy for establishing
(II)L
is to define tuple weight functions for the edges between the vertices
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
and their corresponding target sets
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
, which we will do in this step depending on the three cases whether
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
,
0\in I\setminus J
, and
0\in J_{XV}
.

For all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}=\{\mathscrs{a},\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
with
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})>0
, and
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
as defined in (
5.19
), we make the following definition. (For notational convenience, we treat
\{\emptyset\}
as
\emptyset
in the following definition.)

(5.20)

\displaystyle E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}:=\bigg\{

\displaystyle\{\mathscrs{a}\cap(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0})\}\cup\{e_{x}\}_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}\in\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{\mathbbm{1}\{0\in I\setminus J\}+|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|}^{=}\colon

\displaystyle e_{x}\in E\left(A_{0}^{H}[\{x\},T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}]\right)\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}$}\bigg\}.

Let us explain the definition of
E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
.
If
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, then
\mathscrs{a}\cap(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0})=\emptyset
.
Thus,
E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
is the set of clean
|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}|
-tuples of edges in
E(A_{0}^{H})
between a vertex
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
and its target set
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
.
If
0\in I\setminus J
, then
\mathscrs{a}\cap(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0})=\{x{a},c_{0}\}
.
(Recall that
x{a}=\mathscrs{a}\cap X_{0}^{H}
.)
Thus,
E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
is the set of clean
(1+|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}|)
-tuples of edges in
E(A_{0}^{H})
where we additionally require that the tuple contains the edge
x{a}c_{0}
.
If
0\in J_{XV}
, we will not make use of the definition of
E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
.

With
E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
we can define the following weight function
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\mathbbm{1}\{0\in I\setminus J\}+|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}|}\to[0,s]

by

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}(\mathbf{e}):=\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})\cdot\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathbf{e}\in E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\}}.

The motivation behind this is the following observation for the two cases
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, and
0\in I\setminus J
.
We claim that for
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}=\{\mathscrs{a},\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
with
\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0
, if
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}(M(\sigma))>0
and
\sigma^{+}(x{b})\neq c_{0}
if
0\in J
, then
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{new}:=\{\mathscrs{a}\setminus(\mathscrs{a}\cap(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0})),\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(A_{I_{r}}^{H,new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H,new})
.
To see that this is true, note that if
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}(M(\sigma))>0
, then the definition of the target sets
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
implies that (
4.1
) of Definition
4.1
for the updated candidacy graphs
A^{H,new}_{I_{r}}=A^{H_{+}}_{I_{r}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
and
B_{j}^{H,new}=B_{j}^{H_{+}^{j}}(\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+})
is satisfied.
Hence in this case, for the edge tester
\omega^{new}
as defined in the statement of
(II)L
, we obtain by (
4.7
) of Definition
4.5
of
\omega^{new}
that
\omega^{new}\big({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{new}\big)=\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})
requiring that
\sigma^{+}(x{b})\neq c_{0}
if
0\in J
so that
(v)D
of Definition
4.5
is satisfied.
Note that
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{new}={\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
if
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
.

In order to ensure that
\sigma^{+}(x{b})\neq c_{0}
if
0\in J
, we apply an inclusion-exclusion principle and introduce another weight function
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{-}
that accounts for the weight in the case that
0\in J
and
\sigma^{+}(x{b})=c_{0}
.
To that end, similarly as in (
5.20
),
for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}=\{\mathscrs{a},\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
with
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})>0
, and
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
as defined in (
5.19
), we define the following set of edge tuples

\displaystyle E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{-}:=\bigg\{

\displaystyle\{\mathscrs{b}\cap(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0})\}\cup\{e_{x}\}_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\setminus\{x{b}\}}\in\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{\mathbbm{1}\{0\in J\}+|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\setminus\{x{b}\}|}^{=}\colon

\displaystyle e_{x}\in E\left(A_{0}^{H}[\{x\},T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}]\right)\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\setminus\{x{b}\}$}\bigg\}.

Analogously to
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
, we define the weight function
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{-}\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\mathbbm{1}\{0\in J\}+|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\{x{b}\}|}\to[0,s]

by

\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{-}(\mathbf{e}):=\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})\cdot\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathbf{e}\in E_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{-}\}}
.

The size of the tuple weight functions depends on the cardinality of
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
.
To that end, let

(5.21)

\displaystyle b_{\max}:=\max\{|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}|\colon\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}),\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0\},

and we will group the tuple functions for all
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
with
\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0
into all possible
(\mathbbm{1}\{0\in I\setminus J\}+b)
-tuple weight functions for
b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}
.
To this end, for each
b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}
, we set

(5.22)

\displaystyle\Omega_{b}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\colon\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0,|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}|=b\right\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{b}^{-}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\colon\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0,|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\{x{b}\}|=b\right\},

as well as

\displaystyle\omega_{b}:=\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\omega_{b}^{-}:=\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}^{-}}\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{-}.

In the two cases when
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, and
0\in I\setminus J
, we will see that
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
is the major contribution to
\omega^{new}\big(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\big)
.
However, since
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
is a proper subset of
\mathscr{X}_{0}
, we additionally need to consider those
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})
for which a relevant vertex in
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
has not been embedded by
\sigma
as this might also contribute to the weight of
\omega^{new}
.
This is also the case when
0\in J_{XV}
, because then we require that either
x{b}=\mathscrs{b}\cap X_{0}^{H}
is not embedded or no
H
-vertex is mapped onto the centre
c_{0}
.

We collect some notation.
For all
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})
with
\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0
, let
H_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\mathcal{H}
be such that
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}})
, and let
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}:=\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
.
Recall that
J_{X},J_{V}\subseteq J
are disjoint sets and
J_{XV}=J_{X}\cup J_{V}
.
Let
z{b}:=x{b}
if
0\in J_{X}
, and let
z{b}:=c_{0}
if
0\in J_{V}
.
We can now define the following set of edge tuples that we describe in detail below.
For
b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}
,
\ell\in[b]_{0}
,
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}
, let

(5.23)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}):=\Big\{

\displaystyle{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}\colon|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}|=\ell,\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|=m_{A},\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|=m_{B},

\displaystyle\sigma(x)\in T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}$, }\text{if $0\in I\setminus J$ then $\sigma(x{a})=\{c_{0}\}$,}

\displaystyle\text{if $0\in J_{XV}$ then $z{b}\in(\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma})\cup(V_{0}\setminus\sigma(X_{0}^{H_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}))$}\Big\}.

That is,
\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
is the set of edges
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
such that there exists an
\ell
-set
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}
of vertices in
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
which are not embedded by
\sigma
, and these
\ell
vertices in
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}
contribute
m_{A}
and
m_{B}
many
(k-1)
-sets, and all remaining
b-\ell
vertices
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
are embedded onto their target set
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
.
Additionally, if
0\in I\setminus J
, then we require that
x{a}
is mapped onto
c_{0}
by
\sigma
, and if
0\in J_{X}
, then we require that
x{b}
is not embedded by
\sigma
, and if
0\in J_{V}
, then we require that no vertex of
X_{0}^{H_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
is mapped onto
c_{0}
by
\sigma
.
Further, let

(5.24)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}):=\Big\{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\colon

\displaystyle\sigma^{+}(x)\in V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}$,}

\displaystyle\text{if $x{b}\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}$ then $\sigma^{+}(x{b})\neq c_{0}$}\Big\}.

That is,
\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\subseteq\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
contains those edges
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
, where the not embedded vertices in
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
are nevertheless mapped onto their target set
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
by the random cluster-injective extension
\sigma^{+}
of
\sigma
, and
\sigma^{+}
does not map
x{b}
onto
c_{0}
.
Thus, in such a case the weight of
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
will ‘accidentally’ be taken into account in addition to the ‘real’ contribution given by
\sigma
(compare also with (
5.25
) below).

Crucially note that in the two cases when
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
and
0\in I\setminus J
, we know for
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}=\{\mathscrs{a},\mathscrs{b}\}\in\Omega_{b}
that
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{new}=\{\mathscrs{a}\setminus(\mathscrs{a}\cap(X_{0}^{H}\cup V_{0})),\mathscrs{b}\}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})

only if

\sigma^{+}(x{b})\neq c_{0}
if
0\in J

and
either
\omega_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}(M(\sigma))=\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})>0
or

{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
for some
\ell\in[b]
,
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}
. (Recall that
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}^{new}={\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
if
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
.)
This holds by (
4.1
) of Definition
4.1
and since we defined
\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new}
and
\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new}
in (
5.17
) as updated candidacy graphs with respect to
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
.
(Note that since we choose
\sigma^{+}
as a ‘dummy’ enlargement, we do not require that
\sigma^{+}(x)\in N_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}(x)
, which is the reason why
\sigma^{+}(x)\in V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
in (
5.24
) instead of
\sigma^{+}(x)\in T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
.)
Hence, for the two cases when
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
and
0\in I\setminus J
, we make the following key observation:

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)

(5.25)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\left(\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))-{\mathbbm{1}\{0\in J\setminus J_{XV}\}\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))}\right)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in[b_{\max}],\ell\in[b],\\
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}\end{subarray}}\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right).

In the case that
0\in J_{XV}
, it suffices in view of the statement to establish an upper bound for

\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)
.
Similarly as in (
5.25
), we have in this case for
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
and
b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}
that

\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})

only if

\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
for some
\ell\in[b]_{0}
,
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}
.
This holds by (
4.1
) of Definition
4.1
and since we defined
\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new}
and
\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new}
in (
5.17
) as updated candidacy graphs with respect to
\phi_{\circ}\cup\sigma^{+}
.
Hence, for the case that
0\in J_{XV}
, we make the following key observation:

(5.26)

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in[b_{\max}]_{0},\ell\in[b]_{0},\\
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}\end{subarray}}\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right).

In Steps
5.2
–
5.2
, we will estimate the weight of the contributing terms in (
5.25
) and (
5.26
).
To do so, we first determine the sizes of the target sets
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
and
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
in the next step.

Step 3.2.

Size of the target sets

and

{b\in[b_{\max}]}
and

be fixed.
We observe that

(5.27)

\displaystyle|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|=(1\pm 3{\varepsilon})d_{A}^{|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|}d_{B}^{|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|}n,\quad\text{ and }\quad|T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|=(1\pm 3{\varepsilon})d_{A}^{|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|}d_{B}^{|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|}d_{0}n,

for

,
where we used

and that

-super-regular and

and

.

of an edge tester we require that

because

in

.
Hence, the edges

many such blue edges by the definition of the

For
b_{I_{r}}=\sum_{i\in I_{r}}b_{i}
and

as defined in (
