), we claim that

(5.28)

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|=b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd},\quad\text{ and }\quad\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b},J}|=b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}.

We establish the first equation in (
5.28
); the second one then follows similarly.
At this part of the proof it is crucial to refresh Definition
4.2
because we make use of all the details of the pattern definitions.
Further, recall that
\mathscrs{y}_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}=\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r_{0}}\cup J}:=(\mathscrs{a}\cup\mathscrs{b})\cap X^{H}_{\cup{(I_{r_{0}}\cup J)}}
and thus
x{a}=y_{0}
if
0\in I_{r_{0}}
; otherwise,
x{a}=\emptyset
.
Note that in order to compute
|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|
it is equivalent to count
|E_{x,\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}}|
.
By definition of
\mathcal{H}_{+}^{i}
, we have that
|\bigcup_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}}E_{x,y_{i}}|=b_{i}
for all
i\in(I_{r}\cup J)\setminus\{0\}
(see (
5.10
)).
That is, there are
b_{I_{r}}=\sum_{i\in I_{r}}b_{i}
edges
\mathscrs{e}\in\bigcup_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}}E_{x,\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}}
with
|\mathscrs{e}\cap\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}|=1
.
Out of these
b_{I_{r}}
edges, we claim that
p_{0}^{A,2nd}
edges
\mathscrs{e}
satisfy that
x{a}\in\mathscrs{e}
, that is,
\sum_{i\in I_{r}}|E_{x{a},y_{i}}|=p_{0}^{A,2nd}
.
For an illustration of these edges in
\bigcup_{i\in I_{r}}E_{x{a},y_{i}}
, see Figure
4
.
Indeed, since
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})>0
, we have by Definition
4.4
of an edge tester that
\mathscrs{y}_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\subseteq\mathscrs{x}_{I}
for some
\mathscrs{x}_{I}\in\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I}
with
\mathscrs{x}_{I}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},I,J)
.
Thus, by Definition
4.3
of
E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},I,J)
, we have that
\mathbf{p}^{A}(\mathscrs{x}_{I},J)=\mathbf{p}^{A}=(p_{i}^{A})_{i\in{V(R)}}
and
\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}(\mathscrs{x}_{I},J)=\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}=(p_{i}^{A,2nd})_{i\in{V(R)}}
.
Hence, by the definition of a
2^{nd}
-pattern in Definition
4.2
and because
\mathcal{H}_{+}
is constructed such that each subset
\{x{a},y_{i}\}
only lies in proper edges of
\mathcal{H}
due to
(c)
and (
5.7
),
we have
p_{0}^{A,2nd}=\sum_{i\in I_{r}}|E_{x{a},y_{i}}|
.
By the definition of
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
in (
5.19
) which excludes
x{a}
, this accounts for the term ‘
b_{I_{r}}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}
’ in (
5.28
).
It is worth pointing out that
p_{0}^{A,2nd}=p_{0}^{B,2nd}=0
if
0\notin I_{r_{0}}
because then
x{a}=\emptyset
.

Further, we claim that there are
p_{0}^{A}
edges
\mathscrs{e}\in\bigcup_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}}E_{x,\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}}
with
|\mathscrs{e}\cap\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}|\geq 2
but
x{a}\notin\mathscrs{e}
.
Since
\mathscrs{y}_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\subseteq\mathscrs{x}_{I}
for some
\mathscrs{x}_{I}\in\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I}
with
\mathscrs{x}_{I}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},I,J)
, we have that
\mathbf{p}^{A}(\mathscrs{x}_{I},J)=\mathbf{p}^{A}=(p_{i}^{A})_{i\in{V(R)}}
.
Hence, by the definition of a
1^{st}
-pattern in Definition
4.2
and by (
5.8
), there are
p_{0}^{A}
edges
\mathscrs{e}\in\bigcup_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}}E_{x,\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}}
with
|\mathscrs{e}\cap\{y_{i}\}_{i\in I_{r}}|\geq 2
because of the last two conditions in (
4.3
), and all of these edges
\mathscrs{e}
satisfy that
x{a}\notin\mathscrs{e}
due to the condition ‘
(\mathscrs{f}\cap X^{H}_{\ell})\setminus\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I\setminus J}\neq\emptyset
’ in (
4.3
).
Altogether, this implies (
5.28
).

Hence, for
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
, we have by (
5.27
) and (
5.28
) that

(5.29)

\displaystyle\prod_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|T_{x,\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}|=(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}d_{0}^{b}n^{b}.

Step 4.

Estimating
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
in (
5.25
)

In this step we estimate the contribution of the first term
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
in (
5.25
).
Throughout this step, let us consider the case that
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
.
At the end of the step we explain how the estimate of
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
changes if
0\in I\setminus J
.
Note, if
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, then
\omega_{0}
is the empty function and thus,

\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))=\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
. (That is,
b=0
is only relevant if
0\in I\setminus J
.)

We first consider
\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
for some
{b\in[b_{\max}]}
.
By (
5.29
) and the definition of
\omega_{b}
, we have

(5.30)

\displaystyle\omega_{b}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))=(1\pm 2{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}d_{0}^{b}n^{b}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}).

We verify that
\omega_{b}
satisfies (
5.14
).
For all
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{b^{\prime}}\}\in\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{b^{\prime}},b^{\prime}\in[b]
, the number of edges
\{e_{b^{\prime}+1},\ldots,e_{b}\}
such that
\mathbf{e}=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{b}\}\in\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{b}
with
\omega_{b}(\mathbf{e})>0
is at most
\Delta n^{b-b^{\prime}}
(recall that we have chosen
\Delta
such that
{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\ll 1/\Delta\ll 1/t\ll 1/k,1/r,1/r_{\circ},1/s
),
implying that

\|\omega_{b}\|_{b^{\prime}}\leq\Delta^{2}n^{b-b^{\prime}}\leq n^{b-b^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}^{2}}.

Hence, by adding
\omega_{b}
to
\mathcal{W}
, (
5.16
) implies that

\displaystyle\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:weight w_b}}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{\omega_{b}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))}{(d_{0}n)^{b}}\pm n^{\varepsilon}

(5.31)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:weight w_b}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/2})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})\pm n^{\varepsilon}.

Finally, observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})=\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})=\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right),

and thus (
5.31
) implies that

(5.32)

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))=(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/3})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)\pm n^{2{\varepsilon}},

which is the desired estimate of
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))=\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
in the case that
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
.

Let us now assume that
0\in I\setminus J
and we explain how the estimate of
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))
changes.
(Note that we allow for
b=0
.)
The intuition is that we additionally require that
x{a}
is mapped onto
c_{0}
which we would expect to happen in an idealized random setting with probability roughly
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
.
In fact, (
5.30
) is still true in the case that
0\in I\setminus J
, but note that
\omega_{b}
is now a
(1+b)
-tuple weight function which yields an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
in (
5.31
) and thus also in (
5.32
).
Hence, we obtain (
5.32
) with an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
as the desired estimate in the case that
0\in I\setminus J
.

Step 5.

Estimating
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))
in (
5.25
) if
0\in J\setminus J_{XV}

In this step we establish an upper bound on the contribution of the minuend
\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))
in (
5.25
) and therefore, suppose that
0\in J\setminus J_{XV}
.

We first consider
\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))
for some
{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}
.
It suffices to establish only a rough upper bound which follows directly by the definition of
\omega_{b}^{-}
:

(5.33)

\displaystyle\omega_{b}^{-}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))\leq 2n^{b}\sum_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}^{-}}\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}).

It is easy to verify that
\omega_{b}^{-}
satisfies (
5.14
).
Further, note that
\omega_{b}^{-}
is a
(b+1)
-tuple weight function-
Hence, by adding
\omega_{b}
to
\mathcal{W}
, (
5.16
) implies that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))\leq(1+{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{\omega_{b}^{-}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))}{(d_{0}n)^{b+1}}+n^{\varepsilon}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:weight w_b^-}}}{{\leq}}n^{{\hat{\varepsilon}}-1}\sum_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}^{-}}\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})+n^{\varepsilon}.

Finally, observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}^{-}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})\leq\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right),

and thus (
5.34
) implies that

(5.35)

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))\leq n^{{\hat{\varepsilon}}-1}\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)+n^{2{\varepsilon}}.

Hence, combining (
5.32
) and (
5.35
) in the case that
0\in J\setminus J_{XV}
, yields that

(5.36)

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}}\left(\omega_{b}(M(\sigma))-\omega_{b}^{-}(M(\sigma))\right)=

\displaystyle(1\pm 2{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/3})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}

\displaystyle\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)\pm 2n^{2{\varepsilon}}.

Step 6.

Estimating
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))

In this step we derive an upper bound for

\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
for fixed
b\in[b_{\max}]_{0}
,
\ell\in[b]_{0}
,
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}
, and
\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\supseteq\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
as defined in (
5.23
).
We will use this bound in the subsequent Step
5.2
to derive an upper bound for
\omega(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
as in (
5.25
) and (
5.26
).
Throughout this step, let us again consider the case that
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, and thus
b,\ell>0
. At the end of the step, we explain how the estimate changes if
0\in I\setminus J
or
0\in J_{XV}
.

Our general strategy is based on the following inclusion-exclusion principle.
For every
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
, we estimate the

(5.37)

\omega
-weight
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})
with tuples
\mathscrs{x}\in\binom{\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}{b}
such that
b-\ell
vertices
x
of
\mathscrs{x}
are mapped onto their target set
T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
and the remaining
\ell
vertices
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell}
of
\mathscrs{x}
satisfy

(
\ast
)

\quad\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[\ell]}|\mathcal{S}_{x_{i},{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|=m_{A},\quad\quad\sum_{i\in[\ell]}|\mathcal{S}_{x_{i},{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|=m_{B},

(5.38)

\omega
-weight
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})
as in
(
5.37
)
with tuples
\mathscrs{x}
where we additionally require that the remaining
\ell
vertices vertices
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell}
of
\mathscrs{x}
are embedded by
\sigma
and satisfy
(
\ast
)
.

Now, subtracting (
5.38
) from (
5.37
) yields the

(5.39)

\omega
-weight
\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}})
as in
(
5.37
)
with tuples
\mathscrs{x}
where at least one of the remaining
\ell
vertices
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell}
of
\mathscrs{x}
is not embedded by
\sigma
, and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell}
satisfy
(
\ast
)
.

Hence, summing over the
\omega
-weight as in (
5.39
) for all
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
yields an upper bound for
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
as defined in (
5.23
) when
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
.

First, in order to estimate (
5.37
) and (
5.38
), we define the following sets of tuples of edges in
\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
For all
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
with
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}
, let

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma},m_{A},m_{B}}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\mathscr{X}\in\binom{\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}{\ell}\colon\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|=m_{A},\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|=m_{B}\bigg\};

(5.40)

\displaystyle E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{\mathscr{X}\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma},m_{A},m_{B}}}\bigg\{\{e_{x}\}_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\mathscr{X}}\in\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{b-\ell}^{=}\colon

\displaystyle\hphantom{:=
\bigcup_{\mathscr{X}\in\mathscr{X}_0^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma},m_A,m_B}}
\bigg\lx@text@lbrace }e_{x}\in E(A_{0}^{H}[\{x\},T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}])\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\mathscr{X}$}\bigg\};

(5.41)

\displaystyle E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{\mathscr{X}\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma},m_{A},m_{B}}}\bigg\{\{e_{x}\}_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}\in\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{b}^{=}\colon

\displaystyle\hphantom{:=
\bigg\lx@text@lbrace }x\in e_{x}\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}$, }e_{x}\in E(A_{0}^{H}[\{x\},T_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}])\text{ for all $x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\mathscr{X}$}\bigg\}.

Note that the edges in
E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}
and
E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}
correspond to the described situations in (
5.37
) and (
5.38
), respectively.
We define weight functions
\omega_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}\colon\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{b-\ell}\to[0,s]
and
\omega_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}\colon\binom{E(A_{0}^{H})}{b}\to[0,s]
by

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(\mathbf{e}):=\mathbbm{1}\{\mathbf{e}\in E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}\}\cdot\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}),\quad\text{ and }\quad\omega_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(\mathbf{e}):=\mathbbm{1}\{\mathbf{e}\in E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}\}\cdot\omega({\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}).

Let

\displaystyle\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(\mathbf{e}):=\sum_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(\mathbf{e}),\quad\text{ and }\quad\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(\mathbf{e}):=\sum_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(\mathbf{e}).

We estimate
\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))
and
\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))
.
By (
5.29
) and the definition of
E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}
and
E_{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}
in (
5.40
) and (
5.41
), respectively, we obtain

(5.42)

\displaystyle\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))

\displaystyle=(1\pm 2{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}-m_{A}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}-m_{B}}(d_{0}n)^{b-\ell}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b});

(5.43)

\displaystyle\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))

\displaystyle=(1\pm 2{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}-m_{A}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}-m_{B}}(d_{0}n)^{b}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}).

Again, we can add
\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}
and
\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}
to
\mathcal{W}
and employ property (
5.16
).
This yields that

\displaystyle\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(M(\sigma))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:weight w_b b-l hit}}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))}{(d_{0}n)^{b-\ell}}\pm n^{\varepsilon}

(5.44)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:weight w_b b-l hit}}}{{=}}(1\pm 4{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}-m_{A}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}-m_{B}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})\pm n^{\varepsilon}

and

\displaystyle\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(M(\sigma))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:weight w_b b-l hit, l embedded}}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{0}))}{(d_{0}n)^{b}}\pm n^{\varepsilon}

(5.45)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:weight w_b b-l hit, l embedded}}}{{=}}(1\pm 4{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}-m_{A}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}-m_{B}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})\pm n^{\varepsilon}.

Finally, as observed in (
5.39
), subtracting (
5.45
) from (
5.44
) gives us an upper bound on
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
.
We obtain

\displaystyle\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit}}(M(\sigma))-\omega_{\Gamma}^{\eqref{eq:estimate b-l hit, l embedded}}(M(\sigma))

(5.46)

\displaystyle\leq 8{\hat{\varepsilon}}d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}-m_{A}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}-m_{B}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})+2n^{\varepsilon}.

Let us now first assume that
0\in I\setminus J
and we explain how the estimate on
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
changes.
If
0\in I\setminus J
, then by the definition of
\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
in (
5.23
), we additionally require that
x{a}
is mapped onto
c_{0}
by
\sigma
which we would again expect to happen with probability roughly
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
.
That is, we have to modify the definitions in (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) by additionally adding the edge
x{a}c_{0}
to the tuples.
Again, the estimates for the total weights in (
5.42
) and (
5.43
) are still true but we obtain an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
in (
5.44
) and (
5.45
) as the sizes of the tuple functions increased by
1
.
Thus, we also obtain (
5.46
) with an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
which will be our desired estimate in the case that
0\in I\setminus J
.

Finally, let us assume that
0\in J_{XV}
and we explain how the estimate on
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
changes.
If
0\in J_{XV}
, then by the definition of
\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
in (
5.23
), we additionally require that either
x{b}
is left unembedded by
\sigma
, or no
H^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
-vertex is mapped onto
c_{0}
.
This can be achieved by modifying the definition in (
5.41
) such that the edge tuples are increased by adding the
A_{0}^{H}
-edges
e_{z{b}}
such that
z{b}\in e_{z{b}}
.
This ensures that for
z{b}\in\{x{b},c_{0}\}
, we either have
x{b}
is left unembedded by
\sigma
, or no
H^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
-vertex is mapped onto
c_{0}
.
The modification adds another factor of
d_{0}n
to the total weight in (
5.43
) but also another factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
to (
5.45
).
Thus, (
5.46
) will also be our desired estimate in the case that
0\in J_{XV}
.

Step 7.

Estimating
\omega(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
in (
5.25
) and (
5.26
)

We will use the bounds on
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
of Step
5.2
to derive an upper bound for the
\omega
-weight
\omega(\Gamma^{hit}_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
.
Let us first assume the two cases that
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, and
0\in J_{XV}
, since both cases yield the same bound on
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
in (
5.46
).
The general idea is that we will obtain additional factors ‘
d_{A}^{m_{A}}
’ and ‘
d_{B}^{m_{B}}
’ in (
5.46
) when we extend
\sigma
to
\sigma^{+}
, that is, when we embed the
\ell
unembedded
\mathcal{H}_{+}
-neighbours of each
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
contributing to
\omega(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
.
Only if these
\ell
vertices are mapped onto their target set
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
, then
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}
also contributes to
\omega(\Gamma^{hit}_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
; that is, if
\sigma^{+}(x)\in V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
for all
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
. (Recall the definition of
\Gamma^{hit}_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
in (
5.24
).)
This happens roughly with probability
d_{A}^{m_{A}}d_{B}^{m_{B}}
.
We proceed with the details.

Note that
\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})=\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
for
\ell=0
, and thus we may consider fixed

b\in[b_{\max}]
,
\ell\in[b]
,
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}
with
m_{A}+m_{B}>0
.
Note that we extend
\sigma|_{V(H)}
to
\sigma^{+}|_{V(H)}
for every
H\in\mathcal{H}
by choosing a bijective mapping of
X_{0}^{H}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
into
V_{0}\setminus\sigma(X_{0}^{H}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma})
uniformly and independently at random.
To that end, for
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
,
H\in\mathcal{H}
, and
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
,
let
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}:=V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\setminus\sigma(X_{0}^{H}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma})
.
We first estimate
|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}|
.
To that end, let
\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in E(A_{I_{r_{0}}}^{H})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(B_{j}^{H})
,
H\in\mathcal{H}
, and
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
be fixed.
For every
v\in V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}
, we define a weight function
\omega_{v}\colon E(A_{0}^{H})\to\{0,1\}
by
\omega_{v}(e):=\mathbbm{1}\{v\in e\}
and let
\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}:=\sum_{v\in V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}\omega_{v}
.
Observe that
\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}(M(\sigma))
counts the vertices
v\in V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}\setminus V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}
.
Hence,

(5.47)

\displaystyle|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}|=|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|-\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}(M(\sigma)).

Since
A_{0}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{0})
super-regular, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}(E(A_{0}^{H}))=(1\pm 3{\varepsilon})d_{0}n|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|.

Adding
\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}
to
\mathcal{W}
and employing (
5.15
) yields that

\displaystyle\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}(M(\sigma))=(1\pm{\varepsilon})(1-{\varepsilon}^{2/3})\frac{\omega_{V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}}(E(A_{0}^{H}))}{d_{0}n}\pm n^{\varepsilon}=(1\pm 5{\varepsilon})(1-{\varepsilon}^{2/3})|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|.

We conclude that

(5.48)

\displaystyle|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:size V_x,ab,bars}}}{{\leq}}2{\varepsilon}^{2/3}|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:size V_x,ab}}}{{\leq}}3{\varepsilon}^{2/3}d_{A}^{|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|}d_{B}^{|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|}n.

For
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
, let
\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}:=\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
. By the definition of
\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
in (
5.23
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{J}}|=m_{A},\quad\sum_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}}|\mathcal{S}_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},J}|=m_{B}.

Hence, by (
5.48
) and because
|\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}|=\ell
, we obtain

(5.49)

\displaystyle\prod_{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}}|V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}|\leq d_{A}^{m_{A}}d_{B}^{m_{B}}(3{\varepsilon}^{2/3}n)^{\ell}.

By (
5.18
), we have that
V_{0}\setminus\sigma(X_{0}^{H}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma})\geq{\varepsilon}^{2/3}n/3
.
Now, with (
5.49
) we obtain that the probability that all
\ell
vertices
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}
are mapped onto their target set
V_{x,{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}
and if
x{b}\in\mathscr{X}_{0}^{{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}},\bar{\sigma}}
then it is not mapped onto
c_{0}
— that is, the probability that
{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}}\in\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
is also contained in
\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})
— is at most

\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}^{m_{A}}d_{B}^{m_{B}}(3{\varepsilon}^{2/3}n)^{\ell}}{{\varepsilon}^{2/3}n\cdot({\varepsilon}^{2/3}n-1)\cdots({\varepsilon}^{2/3}n-\ell+1)/3^{\ell}}\leq 10^{\ell}d_{A}^{m_{A}}d_{B}^{m_{B}}.

Finally, we can derive an upper bound for the expected value of
\omega(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B}))
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{\mathbb{E}}\left[\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:bound w-Gamma_b(l,m_A,m_B)}}}}{{\leq}}\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right)10^{\ell}d_{A}^{m_{A}}d_{B}^{m_{B}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:bound w-Gamma_b(l,m_A,m_B)}}}{{\leq}}{\varepsilon}^{1/4}d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})+n^{2{\varepsilon}}.

By using Theorem
3.1
and a union bound, we can establish concentration with probability, say, at least
1-{\rm e}^{-n^{{\varepsilon}}}
.
Thus, we conclude

(5.50)

\displaystyle\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right)\leq 2{\varepsilon}^{1/4}d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}\sum_{\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b}\in\Omega_{b}}\omega(\mathscrs{a}\mathscrs{b})+2n^{2{\varepsilon}}

for all
b\in[b_{\max}]
,
\ell\in[b]
,
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}
with
m_{A}+m_{B}>0
.
By summing over all values of
b,\ell,m_{A},m_{B}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in[b_{\max}],\ell\in[b],\\
m_{A},m_{B}\in[\Delta]_{0}\end{subarray}}\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}^{hit}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right)

(5.51)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:bound w(Gamma^hit)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}^{1/5}d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)+n^{3{\varepsilon}},

which is the desired estimate for the second term in (
5.25
) for the case that
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
, and it is the desired estimate for the right hand side in (
5.26
) for the case that
0\in J_{XV}
(where we also allow
b,\ell=0
in the summation).

In the case that
0\in I\setminus J
, we obtain (
5.51
) with an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
since the estimate on
\omega\left(\Gamma_{b}(\ell,m_{A},m_{B})\right)
from the previous step yields an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
.

Step 8.

Concluding
(II)L
if
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})

Finally, we can establish
(II)L
if
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
by the estimates derived in the Steps
5.2
and
5.2
.
So, let us assume that
0\in(V(R)\setminus I)\cup(J\setminus J_{XV})
.
Using (
5.32
) (respectively (
5.36
) if
0\in J\setminus J_{XV}
) and (
5.51
) in our key observation (
5.25
) yields that

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)=

\displaystyle(1\pm 3{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/3})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}

(5.52)

\displaystyle\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)\pm 2n^{3{\varepsilon}}.

We will use
(P3)
for
\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)
in order to obtain
(II)L
from (
5.52
).
For
0\notin I
or
0\in J\setminus J_{XV}
, we have that

(5.53)

\displaystyle d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}}\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}=\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{A}^{b_{i}}d_{i,A}=\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}

for
d_{i,A}^{new}
defined as in (
5.4
) because
I_{r_{0}}=I_{r}
, and similarly

(5.54)

\displaystyle d_{B}^{b_{J}}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}=\prod_{j\in J}d_{B}^{b_{j}}d_{j,B}=\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new},

because by the definition in (
5.4
), we have
b_{0}=0
and
d_{B}^{b_{0}}=d_{B}^{0}=1
and
d_{0,B}=d_{0,B}^{new}
.
Hence, altogether using
(P3)
together with (
5.52
), we obtain

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}(1\pm 3{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/3})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}}\Bigg(\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}\Big)d_{A}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|}d_{B}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{B}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}}\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}])\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}}\Bigg)\pm 2n^{3{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}{{=}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0}=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2}\Big)d_{A}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}d_{B}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{B}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}}\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0})\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

which establishes
(II)L
in the case of
0\notin I
or
0\in J\setminus J_{XV}
.

Step 9.

Concluding
(II)L
if
0\in I\setminus J

Similar as in Step
5.2
, we can establish
(II)L
if
0\in I\setminus J
by the estimates derived in the Steps
5.2
and
5.2
.
So, let us assume that
0\in I\setminus J
, and recall that we obtained an additional factor of
(d_{0}n)^{-1}
in (
5.32
) and (
5.51
).
Together with our key observation (
5.25
) this yields that

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)

(5.55)

\displaystyle=\quad(1\pm 2{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/3})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}(d_{0}n)^{-1}\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)\pm 2n^{2{\varepsilon}}.

We will use
(P3)
for
\omega\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r_{0}}})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j})\right)
in order to obtain
(II)L
from (
5.55
).
For
0\in I\setminus J
, we have that

(5.56)

\displaystyle d_{0}^{-1}d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}}\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}=\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{A}^{b_{i}}d_{i,A}=\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}

for
d_{i,A}^{new}
defined as in (
5.4
) because
d_{0}=d_{0,A}
, and similarly

(5.57)

\displaystyle d_{B}^{b_{J}}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}=\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new}.

Hence, altogether using
(P3)
together with (
5.55
), we obtain

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}}^{1/3})d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}(d_{0}n)^{-1}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}}\Bigg(\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}\Big)d_{A}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|}d_{B}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{B}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}\Bigg(}\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}])\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}}\Bigg)\pm 2n^{2{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:d_A^new 0 in I},\eqref{eq:d_B^new 0 in I}}}{{=}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0}=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2}\Big)d_{A}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}d_{B}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{B}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}\Big(}\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0})\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

which establishes
(II)L
in the case of
0\in I\setminus J
.

Step 10.

Concluding
(II)L
if
0\in J_{XV}

For the last case that
0\in J_{XV}
, we employ the estimate derived in Step
5.2
in our key observation
5.26
.
For
0\in J_{XV}
, we have that

(5.58)

\displaystyle d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}}\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}=\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{A}^{b_{i}}d_{i,A}=\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}

for
d_{i,A}^{new}
defined as in (
5.4
) because
I_{r_{0}}=I_{r}
, and

(5.59)

\displaystyle d_{B}^{b_{J}}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}=\prod_{j\in J}d_{B}^{b_{j}}d_{j,B}=\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new},

because by the definition in (
5.4
), we have
b_{0}=0
and
d_{B}^{b_{0}}=d_{B}^{0}=1
and
d_{0,B}=d_{0,B}^{new}
.
Hence, altogether using
(P3)
together with (
5.51
) in our key observation (
5.26
), we obtain

\displaystyle\omega^{new}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{r}}^{new})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{new})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{\leq}}{\varepsilon}^{1/5}d_{A}^{b_{I_{r}}+p_{0}^{A}-p_{0}^{A,2nd}}d_{B}^{b_{J}+p_{0}^{B}-p_{0}^{B,2nd}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}}\Bigg(\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap-[r_{\circ}]=\emptyset\}+{\varepsilon}\Big)d_{A}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|}d_{B}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{B}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]}\|}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\eqref{eq:d_A^new},\eqref{eq:d_B^new}}}}{{=}}\Bigg(}\prod_{i\in I_{r_{0}}}d_{i,A}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}])\setminus J|}}+n^{{\varepsilon}}\Bigg)+2n^{2{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:d_A^new 0 in J},\eqref{eq:d_B^new 0 in J}}}{{\leq}}{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2}d_{A}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}d_{B}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{B}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}_{-[r_{\circ}]_{0}}\|}\prod_{i\in I_{r}}d_{i,A}^{new}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j,B}^{new}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap-[r_{\circ}]_{0})\setminus J|}}+n^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}},

which establishes
(II)L
in the case of
0\in J_{XV}
and concludes the proof of
(II)L
.

Step 11.

Checking
(I)L

In order to establish
(I)L
, we fix
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
Z\in\{A,B\}
and
i\in[r]
if
Z=A
,
i\in{V(R)\setminus\{0\}}
if
Z=B
, and we may assume that
i\in N_{R_{\ast}}(0)
otherwise
Z_{i}^{H,new}=Z_{i}^{H}
.
We will first show that
Z_{i}^{H,new}
as defined in (
5.17
) is
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},d_{i}^{new})
-regular by employing Theorem
3.5
.
In order to do so, we verify that every vertex in
X_{i}^{H}
has the appropriate degree in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
and that most pairs of vertices in
X_{i}^{H}
have the appropriate common neighbourhood in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
. These properties follow easily due to the typicality of
G_{Z}
and because
Z_{i}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon},q)
-well-intersecting.
Finally, we show that also each vertex in
V_{i}
has the correct degree in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
by employing
(II)L
. Altogether this will imply that
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},d_{i}^{new})
-super-regular.
Since we basically obtain
Z_{i}^{H,new}
from
Z_{i}^{H}
by restricting the neighbourhood of every vertex by
b_{i}\leq\Delta(R)
additional
(k-1)
-sets in
G_{Z}
(see (
5.60
) and (
5.61
) below), we will obtain directly from
(P1)
that
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},q+\Delta(R))
-well-intersecting.
We proceed with the details.

For every vertex
y\in X_{i}^{H}
, let

(5.60)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{y}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\phi_{\circ}(\mathscrs{e})\cup\sigma^{+}(x)\colon\mathscrs{e}\in E_{x,y},x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}\right\}

with
E_{x,y}
defined as in (
5.9
).
Note that
\mathcal{S}_{y}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)\colon i\in\mathscrs{r}}V_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}\setminus\{i\}}
and
|\mathcal{S}_{y}|=b_{i}
.
Since
G_{Z}
is
({\varepsilon},t,d_{Z})
typical with respect to
R
by
(P1)
, and since
Z_{i}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{i,Z})
-super-regular and
({\varepsilon},q)
-well-intersecting by
(P2)
, we conclude by the definition of
Z_{i}^{H,new}
as in (
5.17
) that for all
y\in X_{i}^{H}
, we have

(5.61)

\displaystyle\deg_{Z^{H,new}_{i}}(y)=|N_{Z^{H}_{i}}(y)\cap N_{G_{Z}}(\mathcal{S}_{y})|=(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}}|V_{i}|.

Note that (
5.61
) implies in particular that the density of
X_{i}^{H}
and
V_{i}
in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is
d_{Z_{i}^{H,new}}(X_{i}^{H},V_{i})=(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}}
.

We can proceed similarly as for the conclusion (
5.61
) and obtain that all but at most
n^{3/2}
pairs
\{y,y^{\prime}\}\in\binom{X_{i}^{H}}{2}
satisfy

(5.62)

\displaystyle|N_{Z^{H,new}_{i}}(y\land y^{\prime})|=(1\pm{\hat{\varepsilon}})(d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}})^{2}|V_{i}|.

To see (
5.62
), note that

\displaystyle N_{Z^{H,new}_{i}}(y\land y^{\prime})=N_{Z^{H}_{i}}(y\land y^{\prime})\cap N_{G_{Z}}(\mathcal{S}_{y})\cap N_{G_{Z}}(\mathcal{S}_{y^{\prime}}),

and for all but at most
2\Delta_{R}n
pairs
\{y,y^{\prime}\}\in\binom{X_{i}^{H}}{2}
, we have
|\mathcal{S}_{y}\cup\mathcal{S}_{y^{\prime}}|=2b_{i}
by (
5.6
).
By employing again
(P1)
and
(P2)
, we obtain (
5.62
), where we used (
4.2
) that for all but at most
2n\cdot n^{1/4+{\varepsilon}}
pairs
\{y,y^{\prime}\}\in\binom{X_{i}^{H}}{2}
the sets of
(k-1)
-sets corresponding to
N_{Z_{i}^{H}}(y)
and
N_{Z_{i}^{H}}(y^{\prime})
are disjoint.
Hence, all but at most
2n^{5/4+{\varepsilon}}+2\Delta_{R}n\leq n^{3/2}
pairs
\{y,y^{\prime}\}\in\binom{X_{i}^{H}}{2}
satisfy (
5.62
).

We can now easily derive an upper bound for the number of
4
-cycles in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
by (
5.62
).
To that end, note that every pair
\{y,y^{\prime}\}\in\binom{X_{i}^{H}}{2}
together with a pair of common neighbours in
N_{Z^{H,new}_{i}}(y\land y^{\prime})
forms a
4
-cycle in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
.
Hence, by (
5.62
), the number of
4
-cycles in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is at most

\displaystyle C_{4}(Z_{i}^{H,new})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|X_{i}^{H}|^{2}}{2}\cdot(1+2{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{(d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}})^{4}|V_{i}|^{2}}{2}+n^{3/2}\cdot n^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+3{\hat{\varepsilon}})\frac{(d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}})^{4}|X_{i}^{H}|^{2}|V_{i}|^{2}}{4}.

Thus, we can apply Theorem
3.5
and obtain that

(5.63)

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{H,new}\text{ is $({\varepsilon}^{\prime},d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}})$-regular.}

For every
v\in V_{i}
, in order to control the degree of
v
in
Z^{H,new}_{i}
, we define weight functions
\omega_{v}\colon E(Z_{i}^{H})\to\{0,1\}
by
\omega_{v}(x_{i}v_{i}):=\mathbbm{1}{\{v_{i}=v\}}
,
and
\omega_{\iota}\colon\mathscr{X}_{i}\to\{0,1\}
by
\omega_{\iota}:=\mathbbm{1}{\{x\in X_{i}^{H}\}}
,
and add the
1
-edge tester

(\omega_{v},\omega_{\iota},J=J_{Z},J_{X}=\emptyset,J_{V}=\emptyset,\mathscrs{c}=\{v\},\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}))

to
\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{\prime}
for
J_{A}:=\emptyset
and
J_{B}:=\{i\}
.
This is indeed a (general)
1
-edge tester satisfying Definition
4.4
.
In particular,
\mathbf{p}^{A}(x)=\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}(x)=\mathbf{p}^{B}(x)=\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}(x)=\mathbf{0}
for every
x\in X_{i}^{H}
by Definition
4.2
because the
1^{st}
-pattern and
2^{nd}
-pattern of a single vertex is always
\mathbf{0}
.
Since
Z_{i}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon},d_{i,Z})
-super-regular, we have that

\displaystyle\omega_{v}(E(Z_{i}^{H}))=(1\pm{\varepsilon})d_{i,Z}|X_{i}^{H}|.

Hence in particular, this general edge tester satisfies
(P3)
.
By
(II)L
, we obtain

\displaystyle\dg_{Z^{H,new}_{i}}(v)=\omega_{v}^{new}(E(Z^{H,new}_{i}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{emb lem:general edge testers}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\varepsilon}^{\prime 2})d_{i,Z}^{new}|X_{i}^{H}|\pm n^{{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}.

Together with (
5.61
) and (
5.63
), this implies that
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},d_{i,Z}^{new})
-super-regular because
d_{i,Z}^{new}=d_{i,Z}d_{Z}^{b_{i}}
(see (
5.4
)).
Further, since the neighbourhood of every vertex
y\in X_{i}^{H}
in
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is the intersection of a set
\mathcal{S}_{y}^{new}
of
(k-1)
-sets in
G_{Z}
(see (
5.61
)), and every
y\in X_{i}^{H}
is contained in at most
n^{1/4+{\varepsilon}}+\Delta_{R}
pairs
\{y,y^{\prime}\}\in\binom{X_{i}^{H}}{2}
such that
\mathcal{S}_{y}^{new}\cap\mathcal{S}_{y^{\prime}}^{new}\neq\emptyset
,
we also obtain that
Z_{i}^{H,new}
is
({\varepsilon}^{\prime},q+\Delta(R))
-well-intersecting as defined in (
4.2
).
This establishes
(I)L
.

Step 12.

Checking
(III)L

For every
\omega\in\mathcal{W}_{local}
with
\omega\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{0})}{\ell}^{=}\to[0,s]
, we add
\omega
to
\mathcal{W}
.
Hence, (
5.16
) yields
(III)L
.

Step 13.

Checking
(IV)L

In order to establish
(IV)L
, we fix
(\omega,c)\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
with
\omega\colon\mathscr{X}_{0}\to[0,s]
and
c\in V_{0}
.
By (
5.18
), we have that
V_{0}\setminus\sigma(X_{0}^{H}\cap\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma})\geq{\varepsilon}^{2/3}n/3
for every
H\in\mathcal{H}
, and thus, the probability for a vertex
x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}
to be mapped onto
c
is at most
3/{\varepsilon}^{2/3}n
.
We therefore expect that
\omega\left(\{x\in\mathscr{X}_{0}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{0}^{\sigma}\colon\sigma^{+}(x)=c\}\right)\leq\omega(\mathscr{X}_{0})/n^{1-2{\varepsilon}}
.
By an application of Theorem
3.1
and a union bound, we can establish concentration with probability, say at least
1-{\rm e}^{-n^{{\varepsilon}}}
.
This establishes
(IV)L
and completes the proof of Lemma
5.1
.

\square

6.
Iterative packing

In this section we essentially prove our main result, Theorem
1.5
.
We prove the following lemma whose statement is very similar
because we only require additionally that for every graph
H\in\mathcal{H}
and every reduced edge
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
, the graph
H[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]
is a matching.
This reduction can be achieved by an application of Lemma
3.9
and simplifies several arguments;
it is presented in the proof of Theorem
1.5
in Section
7
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
1/n\ll{\varepsilon}\ll 1/t\ll\alpha,1/k
and
r\leq n^{2\log n}
as well as
d\geq n^{-{\varepsilon}}
.
Suppose
(\mathcal{H},G,R,\mathcal{X},\mathcal{V})
is an
({\varepsilon},t,d)
-typical and
\alpha^{-1}
-bounded blow-up instance of size
(n,k,r)

and
|\mathcal{H}|\leq n^{2k}
.
Suppose that
e_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}})\leq(1-\alpha)dn^{k}
for all
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
,
and
H[X_{\cup\mathscrs{r}}^{H}]
is a matching
if
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
and empty if
\mathscrs{r}\in\binom{[r]}{k}\setminus E(R)
for each
H\in\mathcal{H}
.
Suppose
\mathcal{W}_{set},\mathcal{W}_{ver}
are sets of
\alpha^{-1}
-set testers and
\alpha^{-1}
-vertex testers of size at most
n^{3\log n}
, respectively.
Then there is a packing
\phi
of
\mathcal{H}
into
G
such that

(i)

\phi(X_{i}^{H})=V_{i}
for all
i\in[r]
and
H\in\mathcal{H}
;

(ii)

|W\cap\bigcap_{j\in[\ell]}\phi(Y_{j})|=|W||Y_{1}|\cdots|Y_{\ell}|/n^{\ell}\pm\alpha n
for all
(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}_{set}
;

(iii)

\omega(\phi^{-1}(\mathscrs{c}))=(1\pm\alpha)\omega(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I})/n^{|I|}\pm n^{\alpha}
for all
(\omega,\mathscrs{c})\in\mathcal{W}_{ver}
with centres
\mathscrs{c}
in
I
.

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

We split the proof into five steps.
In Step
6
, we define a vertex colouring of the reduced graph which will incorporate in which order we consider the clusters in turn.
In Step
6
, we partition
G
into two edge-disjoint subgraphs
G_{A}
and
G_{B}
.
In Step
6
, we introduce candidacy graphs and edge testers that we track for the partial packing in Step
6
, where we iteratively apply Lemma
5.1
and consider the clusters in turn with respect to the ordering of the clusters given by the colouring obtained in Step
6
. We only use the edges of
G_{A}
for the partial packing in Step
6
such that we can complete the packing in Step
6
using the edges of
G_{B}
.

Step 1.

Notation and colouring of the reduced graph

We will proceed cluster by cluster in Step
6
to find a function that packs almost all vertices of
\mathcal{H}
into
G
.
Since we allow
r
to grow with
n
and only require that
r\leq n^{\log n}
, we need to carefully control the growth of the error term.
Recall that
R_{\ast}
is the
2
-graph with vertex set
V(R)
and edge set
\bigcup_{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)}\binom{\mathscrs{r}}{2}
.
Let
c\colon V(R)\to[T]
be a proper vertex colouring of
R_{\ast}^{3}
where
T:=k^{3}\alpha^{-3}
.
The colouring naturally yields an order in which we consider the clusters in turn.
To this end, we simply relabel the cluster indices such that the colour values are non-decreasing; that is,

c(1)\leq\cdots\leq c(r)
.
Note that the sets
(c^{-1}(j))_{j\in[T]}
are independent in
R_{\ast}^{3}
.
We choose new constants
{\varepsilon}_{0},{\varepsilon}_{1},\ldots,{\varepsilon}_{T},\mu,\gamma
such that

{\varepsilon}\ll{\varepsilon}_{0}\ll{\varepsilon}_{1}\ll\cdots\ll{\varepsilon}_{T}\ll\mu\ll\gamma\ll 1/t\ll\alpha,1/k.

For
i,q\in[r]
and
I\subseteq[r]
, we define counters
c_{i}(q),c_{I}(q),m_{i}(q)
(see (
6.1
)–(
6.3
) below).
Our intuition is the following:
If we think of
[q]
as the indices of clusters that have already been embedded, then
c_{i}(q)
is the largest colour of an already embedded cluster in the closed neighbourhood of
i
in
R_{\ast}
.
That is,
c_{i}(q)
is the largest colour that is relevant to
i
after embedding the first
q
clusters, and
c_{i}(q)
will incorporate how to update the error term.

To be more precise, for
i,q\in[r]
and an index set
I\subseteq[r]
(that is,
I\subseteq\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
), let

(6.1)

\displaystyle c_{i}(q)

\displaystyle:=\max\left\{\{0\}\cup\{c(j)\colon j\in N_{R_{\ast}}[i]\cap[q]\}\right\};

(6.2)

\displaystyle c_{I}(q)

\displaystyle:=\max_{i\in I}\left\{0,c_{i}(q)\right\}.

Similarly as
c_{i}(q)
, we define
m_{i}(q)
as the number of edges in
R
that contain
i
and where
k-1
clusters excluding
i
have already been embedded.
That is, for
i,q\in[r]
, let

(6.3)

\displaystyle m_{i}(q)

\displaystyle:=\left|\left\{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)\colon i\in\mathscrs{r},|\mathscrs{r}\cap[q]\setminus\{i\}|=k-1\right\}\right|.

Further, for all
i\in[r]
and index sets
I\subseteq[r]
, we set
c_{i}(0)=c_{I}(0)=m_{i}(0):=0
.

For
q\in[r]
, recall that
\mathscr{X}_{q}=\textstyle\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}X^{H}_{q}
, and we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{q}

\displaystyle:=\textstyle\bigcup_{\ell\in[q]}\mathscr{X}_{\ell},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{q}

\displaystyle:=\textstyle\bigcup_{\ell\in[q]}V_{\ell}.

Step 2.

Partitioning the edges of
G

In order to reserve an exclusive set of edges for the completion in Step
6
, we partition the edges of
G
into two subgraphs
G_{A}
and
G_{B}
.
For each edge
\mathscrs{g}
of
G
independently, we add
\mathscrs{g}
to
G_{B}
with probability
\gamma
and otherwise to
G_{A}
.
Let
d_{A}:=(1-\gamma)d
and
d_{B}:=\gamma d
.
Using Chernoff’s inequality and a union bound, we can easily conclude that with probability at least
1-1/n
it holds that

(6.4)

for all
i\in[r]
and all pairs of disjoint sets
\mathcal{S}_{A},\mathcal{S}_{B}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)\colon i\in\mathscrs{r}}V_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}\setminus\{i\}}
with
|\mathcal{S}_{A}\cup\mathcal{S}_{B}|\leq t
, we have

\big|V_{i}\cap N_{G_{A}}(\mathcal{S}_{A})\cap N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S}_{B})\big|=(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{0})d_{A}^{|\mathcal{S}_{A}|}d_{B}^{|\mathcal{S}_{B}|}n.

Hence, we may assume that
G
is partitioned into
G_{A}
and
G_{B}
such that (
6.4
) holds.
In particular, (
6.4
) implies that
G_{Z}
is
(2{\varepsilon}_{0},t,d_{Z})
-typical with respect to
R
for both
Z\in\{A,B\}
.

Step 3.

Partial packings, candidacy graphs and edge testers

For
q\in[r]
, we call
\phi\colon\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[q]}\widehat{X}^{H}_{i}\to\mathcal{V}_{q}
a
q
-partial packing
if
\widehat{X}^{H}_{i}\subseteq X^{H}_{i}
and
\phi(\widehat{X}^{H}_{i})\subseteq V_{i}
for all
H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[q]
such that
\phi
is a packing of
(H[\widehat{X}^{H}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{X}^{H}_{q}])_{H\in\mathcal{H}}
into
G_{A}[\mathcal{V}_{q}]
. Note that
\phi|_{\widehat{X}^{H}_{i}}
is injective for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
i\in[q]
.
For convenience, we often write

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{q}^{\phi}:=\textstyle\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[q]}\widehat{X}^{H}_{i}.

Further, we call
\phi^{+}\colon\mathcal{X}_{q}\to\mathcal{V}_{q}
a
cluster-injective extension
of
\phi
if
\phi^{+}
is an extension of
\phi
such that
\phi^{+}|_{X_{i}^{H}}
is injective (and thus bijective) and
\phi^{+}(X_{i}^{H})=V_{i}
for all
H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[q]
.
Note that we do not even require that
\phi^{+}
is an embedding of
H[X^{H}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup X^{H}_{q}]
for
H\in\mathcal{H}
.

Suppose
q\in[r]
and
\phi_{q}\colon\mathcal{X}_{q}^{\phi_{q}}\to\mathcal{V}_{q}
is a
q
-partial packing with a cluster-injective extension
\phi_{q}^{+}
.
We consider two kinds of candidacy graphs as in Definition
4.1
:
Candidacy graphs
A^{H}_{I}(\phi_{q}^{+})
with respect to
\phi_{q}^{+}
and
G_{A}
for all index sets
I\subseteq[r]\setminus[q]
,
and candidacy graphs
B^{H}_{j}(\phi_{q}^{+})
with respect to
\phi_{q}^{+}
and
G_{B}
for all
j\in[r]
.
The candidacy graphs
A^{H}_{I}(\phi_{q}^{+})
will be used to extend the
q
-partial packing
\phi_{q}
to a
(q+1)
-partial packing
\phi_{q+1}
via Lemma
5.1
in Step
6
, whereas the candidacy graphs
B^{H}_{j}(\phi_{r}^{+})
will be used for the completion in Step
6
.

Given
\phi_{q}
,
\phi_{q}^{+}
and a collection
\mathcal{A}^{q}
and
\mathcal{B}^{q}
of candidacy graphs
A_{I}^{H,q}\subseteq A_{I}^{H}(\phi_{q}^{+})
and
B_{j}^{H,q}\subseteq B_{j}^{H}(\phi_{q}^{+})
for all index sets
I\subseteq[r]\setminus[q]
and
j\in[r]
, we introduce (general) edge testers as in Definition
4.5
to track several quantities during our packing procedure.

To that end, we first define a set
\mathcal{W}_{initial}
of tuples
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
. We also define a superset
\mathcal{W}_{hit}
of
\mathcal{W}_{ver}
containing tuples
(\omega,\mathscrs{c})
.
For every vertex tester
(\omega_{ver},\mathscrs{c})\in\mathcal{W}_{ver}
as in the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
with centres
\mathscrs{c}\in V_{\sqcup I}
for an index set
I\subseteq[r]
, and for all
J\subseteq I
and pairs of disjoint sets
J_{X},J_{V}\subseteq J
,
all
\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]^{r}_{0}
, we define a tuple

(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
with
\omega_{\iota}\colon\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I}\to[0,\alpha^{-1}]
,
\mathscrs{p}:=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})
, by

(6.5)

\displaystyle\omega_{\iota}(\mathscrs{x}):=\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathscrs{x}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},I,J)\}}\cdot\omega_{ver}(\mathscrs{x}),

and we add this tuple to
\mathcal{W}_{initial}
. (Recall Definition
4.3
for
E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},I,J)
.) We also add
(\omega_{ver},\mathscrs{c})
to
\mathcal{W}_{hit}
.

Similarly, for every
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
, all
J\subseteq\mathscrs{r}
and pairs of disjoint sets
J_{X},J_{V}\subseteq J
,
\mathscrs{g}\in V_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}}
, and
\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]^{r}_{0}
, we define a tuple
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{p})
with
\omega_{\iota}\colon\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}}\to\{0,1\}
,
\mathscrs{p}:=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})
, by

(6.6)

\displaystyle\omega_{\iota}(\mathscrs{x}):=\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathscrs{x}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},\mathscrs{r},J)\}},

and we add this tuple to
\mathcal{W}_{initial}
, and
we define a tuple
(\omega,\mathscrs{g})
with
\omega\colon\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{\mathscrs{r}}}\to\{0,1\}
by
\omega(\mathscrs{x}):=\mathbbm{1}\{\mathscrs{x}\in E(\mathcal{H})\}
and add
(\omega,\mathscrs{g})
to
\mathcal{W}_{hit}
.

To control the number of unembedded
H
-vertices in one graph
H
that could potentially be mapped onto a fixed vertex
v
during the completion, we define for all
j\in[r]
,
H\in\mathcal{H}
, and
v\in V_{j}
, a tuple
(\omega_{\iota,H},J=\{j\},J_{X}=\{j\},J_{V}=\emptyset,\mathscrs{c}=\{v\},(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}))
with
\omega_{\iota,H}\colon\mathscr{X}_{j}\to\{0,1\}
by

(6.7)

\displaystyle\omega_{\iota,H}(\mathscrs{x}):=\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathscrs{x}\in X_{j}^{H}\}},

and we add this tuple to
\mathcal{W}_{initial}
.

For one single graph
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
we also consider tuples with only two centres.
That is, for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)
, distinct
j,j_{X}\in\mathscrs{r}
,
v\in V_{j}
,
w\in V_{j_{X}}
, and
\mathbf{p}^{A}=\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}=\mathbf{0},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]^{r}_{0}
, we define
I=J:=\{j,j_{X}\}
,
J_{X}:=\{j_{X}\}
,
J_{V}:=\emptyset
and a tuple
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\{v,w\},\mathscrs{p})
with
\omega_{\iota}\colon\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup I}\to\{0,1\}
,
\mathscrs{p}:=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})
, by

(6.8)

\displaystyle\omega_{\iota}(\mathscrs{x}):=\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathscrs{x}\in E_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathscrs{p},I,J),\mathscrs{x}\subseteq V(H)\}},

and we add this tuple to
\mathcal{W}_{initial}
.

We now define a set
\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{q}=\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{q}(\phi_{q},\phi_{q}^{+},\mathcal{A}^{q},\mathcal{B}^{q})
of edge testers with respect to the elements in
\mathcal{W}_{initial}
.
That is, for every
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})\in\mathcal{W}_{initial}
, let
(\omega_{q},\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
be the edge tester with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
,
(\phi_{q},\phi_{q}^{+})
,
\mathcal{A}^{q}
and
\mathcal{B}^{q}
as in Definition
4.5
, and we add
(\omega_{q},\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
to
\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{q}
.

Step 4.

Induction

We inductively prove that the following statement
\mathbf{S}(q)
holds for all
q\in[r]_{0}
, which will provide a partial packing of
\mathcal{H}
into
G_{A}
.

\mathbf{S}(q)
.

For all
H\in\mathcal{H}
, there exists a
q
-partial packing
\phi_{q}\colon\mathcal{X}_{q}^{\phi_{q}}\to\mathcal{V}_{q}
with
|\mathcal{X}_{q}^{\phi_{q}}\cap X^{H}_{i}|\geq(1-{\varepsilon}_{c_{i}(q)})n
for all
i\in[q]
, and with a cluster-injective extension
\phi_{q}^{+}
of
\phi_{q}
,
and for all index sets
I_{A}\subseteq[r]\setminus[q]
,
I_{B}\in[r]
, and
(Z,\mathcal{Z})\in\{(A,\mathcal{A}),(B,\mathcal{B})\}
, there exist subgraphs
Z^{H,q}_{I_{Z}}
of the candidacy graphs
Z^{H}_{I_{Z}}(\phi_{q}^{+})
with respect to
\phi_{q}^{+}
and
G_{Z}
(where
\mathcal{Z}_{I_{Z}}^{q}:=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H}}Z_{I_{Z}}^{H,q}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{q}
is the collection of all
\mathcal{Z}_{I_{Z}}^{q}
) such that

(a)

Z^{H,q}_{i_{Z}}
is
({\varepsilon}_{c_{i_{Z}}(q)},d_{Z}^{m_{i_{Z}}(q)})
-super-regular and
({\varepsilon}_{c_{i_{Z}}(q)},c_{i_{Z}}(q)t^{1/2})
-well-intersecting with respect to
G_{Z}
for all
i_{A}\in[r]\setminus[q]
,
i_{B}\in[r]
and
Z\in\{A,B\}
;

(b)

for every edge tester
(\omega_{q},\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})\in\mathcal{W}^{q}_{edge}(\phi_{q},\mathcal{A}^{q},\mathcal{B}^{q})
with centres
\mathscrs{c}\in V_{\sqcup I}
for
I\subseteq[r]
, non-empty
I_{q}:=(I\setminus[q])\cup J
, patterns
\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})
, and with
J_{XV}:=J_{X}\cup J_{V}
, we have that

\displaystyle\omega_{q}\left(E(\mathcal{A}_{I_{q}\setminus J}^{q})\sqcup\textstyle\bigsqcup_{j\in J}E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{q})\right)=

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}\cap[q]=\emptyset\}\pm{\varepsilon}_{c_{I_{q}}(q)}\Big)\prod_{Z\in\{A,B\}}d_{Z}^{\|\mathbf{p}^{Z}_{[q]}\|-\|\mathbf{p}^{Z,2nd}_{[q]}\|}

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I_{q}\setminus J}d_{A}^{m_{i}(q)}\prod_{j\in J}d_{B}^{m_{j}(q)}\frac{\omega_{\iota}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|(I\cap[q])\setminus J|}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{c_{I_{q}}(q)}};

(c)

for all

,

,

for all

and all non-empty

\displaystyle\leq\omega(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})/n^{|J\cap[q]|-{\varepsilon}_{c_{J\setminus[q]}(q)}}+n^{{\varepsilon}_{c_{J\setminus[q]}(q)}};

(e)

\big|(V_{i}\setminus\phi_{q}(X_{i}^{H}))\cap N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S})\big|\leq{\varepsilon}_{T}\big|V_{i}\cap N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S})\big|

Step 4.1.

Step 4.2.

Checking
\mathbf{S}(q+1)

In order to establish

(b)
, we fix

(b)

In this case, note in particular that if

Hence, we may assume that

for some

with

.
It is important to note that the edge tester

Step 4.3.

Checking
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(c)

In order to establish
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(c)
, we fix
\mathscrs{g}=\{v_{i_{1}},\ldots,v_{i_{k}}\},\mathscrs{h}=\{w_{j_{1}},\ldots,w_{j_{k}}\}\in E(G)

,

If

(c)
.

Hence, by symmetry, we may assume that

for some

.
Our strategy is to define a weight function that bounds from above the number of elements in

that still can be present in

For
(\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f})\in E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q}}(\mathcal{A}^{q})
, let
\{x_{i_{\ell}}\}:=\mathscrs{e}\cap\mathscr{X}_{i_{\ell}}
, and we define a weight function
\omega_{\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f}}\colon E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})\to\{0,1\}
by
\omega_{\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f}}(xv):=\mathbbm{1}_{\{xv=x_{i_{\ell}}v_{i_{\ell}}\}}
.
Note that
(\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f})\in E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q+1}}(\mathcal{A}^{q+1})
only if
\omega_{\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f}}(M)=1
as it is necessary that
\sigma
embeds
x_{i_{\ell}}
onto
v_{i_{\ell}}
.
Let
\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}:=\sum_{(\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f})\in E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q}}(\mathcal{A}^{q})}\omega_{\mathscrs{e},\mathscrs{f}}.

A key observation is that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\left|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q+1}}(\mathcal{A}^{q+1})\right|\leq\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}(M).

By the definition of
\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}
, we have that

(6.13)

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))=|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q}}(\mathcal{A}^{q})|.

By adding
\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}
to
\mathcal{W}_{local}
and by employing
(III)L
, we obtain with (
6.13
) that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}(M)=(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}^{2})(d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n)^{-1}|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q}}(\mathcal{A}^{q})|\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}.

We further observe that

\displaystyle|E_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h},\phi_{q}}(\mathcal{A}^{q})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{$\mathbf{S}(q)$\ref{e,f suitable}}}}{{\leq}}\max\left\{n^{k-\left|(I\cup J)\cap[q]\right|+{\varepsilon}_{q}^{\ast}},n^{{\varepsilon}_{q}^{\ast}}\right\},

and thus by (
6.10
), (
6.14
) and because
d\geq n^{-{\varepsilon}}
, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{h}}(M)\leq\max\left\{n^{k-\left|(I\cup J)\cap[q]\right|-1+{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}},n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}}\right\}.

By our key observation (
6.12
), this establishes
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(c)
.

Step 4.4.

Checking
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(d)

In order to establish
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(d)
, we fix
(\omega,\mathscrs{c})\in\mathcal{W}_{hit}
with
\mathscrs{c}\in V_{\sqcup I}
and
J\subseteq I
.
In view of the statement, we may assume that
q+1\in J
, otherwise
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(d)
holds by
\mathbf{S}(q)
(d)
.
Our general strategy is to define two weight functions and employ
(III)L
and
(IV)L
to derive the desired upper bound.

For
p\in\{q,q+1\}
, let
W_{p}:=\{\mathscrs{x}\in\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}}\colon\mathscrs{x}\subseteq\mathscrs{e}\text{ for some $\mathscrs{e}\in E(\mathcal{H})$, }\phi_{p}^{+}(\mathscrs{x}\cap\mathscr{X}_{\cup(J\cap[p])})\subseteq\mathscrs{c}\}
, and let
c:=\mathscrs{c}\cap V_{q+1}
.
We define a local tester
\omega_{\sigma}\colon E(\mathcal{A}^{q+1})\to[0,\alpha^{-1}]
by

\omega_{\sigma}(uv):=\sum_{\mathscrs{x}\in W_{q}\colon u\in\mathscrs{x}}\mathbbm{1}\{v=c\}\omega(\mathscrs{x}),

and we add
\omega_{\sigma}
to
\mathcal{W}_{local}
.
We also define a tuple
(\omega_{\sigma^{+}},c)
with
\omega_{\sigma^{+}}\colon\mathscr{X}_{q+1}\to[0,\alpha^{-1}]
by

\omega_{\sigma^{+}}(u):=\sum_{\mathscrs{x}\in W_{q}\colon u\in\mathscrs{x}}\omega(\mathscrs{x}),

and we add
(\omega_{\sigma^{+}},c)
to
\mathcal{W}_{0}
.
We make the following observation

(6.15)

\displaystyle\omega(W_{q+1})=\omega_{\sigma}(M)+\omega_{\sigma^{+}}(\{x\in\mathscr{X}_{q+1}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{q+1}^{\sigma}\colon\sigma^{+}(x)=c\}).

We first employ
(III)L
to derive an upper bound on
\omega_{\sigma}(M)

\displaystyle\omega_{\sigma}(M)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm\ref{emb lem:local weight}}}{{\leq}}2(d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n)^{-1}\omega_{\sigma}(E(\mathcal{A}^{q+1}))+n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\penalty\ $\mathbf{S}(q)$\ref{treffer auf g}\penalty\ }}}{{\leq}}2(d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n)^{-1}\frac{\omega(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|J\cap[q]|-{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}}+2n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}

(6.16)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\rm\ref{emb lem:local weight}}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\omega(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|J\cap[q+1]|-{\varepsilon}^{1/2}_{c(q+1)-1}}}+2n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}.

Next, we employ
(IV)L
to derive an upper bound for the last term of (
6.15
)

\displaystyle\omega_{\sigma^{+}}(\{x\in\mathscr{X}_{q+1}\setminus\mathscr{X}_{q+1}^{\sigma}\colon\sigma^{+}(x)=c\})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{emb lem:hit tester}}}{{\leq}}\omega_{\sigma^{+}}(\mathscr{X}_{q+1})/n^{1-{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}+n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}

(6.17)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\penalty\ $\mathbf{S}(q)$\ref{treffer auf g}\penalty\ }}}{{\leq}}\frac{\omega(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|J\cap[q+1]|-2{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}}+2n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)-1}}.

Finally, plugging (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) into (
6.15
), yields that

\displaystyle\omega(W_{q+1})\leq\frac{\omega(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|J\cap[q+1]|-{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}}}+n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}}.

Together with (
6.10
), this establishes
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(d)
.

Step 4.5.

Checking
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(e)

In order to establish
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(e)
, we fix
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathscrs{r}\in E(R)\colon q+1\in\mathscrs{r}}V_{\sqcup\mathscrs{r}\setminus\{q+1\}}
with
|\mathcal{S}|\leq t
.
Let
W:=V_{q+1}\cap N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S})
.
Our general strategy is to define a weight function that estimates the number of vertices in
W\cap\sigma(X_{q+1}^{H})
from which we can derive an upper bound for
|W\setminus\sigma(X_{q+1}^{H})|
.

Hence, let
\omega_{W}\colon E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})\to\{0,1\}
be defined by
\omega_{W}(e):=\mathbbm{1}\{w\in e,w\in W\}
.
A key observation is that

(6.18)

\displaystyle|W\setminus\sigma(X_{q+1}^{H})|\leq|W|-\omega_{W}(M).

By the definition of
\omega_{W}
and because
A_{q+1}^{H,q}
is
({\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)},d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)})
-super-regular for every
H\in\mathcal{H}
by
\mathbf{S}(q)
(a)
, we have that

(6.19)

\displaystyle\omega_{W}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))=(1\pm 3{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)})d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n|W|.

By adding
\omega_{W}
to
\mathcal{W}_{local}
and by employing
(III)L
, we obtain with (
6.19
) that

\displaystyle\omega_{W}(M)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{emb lem:local weight}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}^{2})\frac{\omega_{W}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))}{d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:weight omega_W}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{T})|W|.

Now, this together with (
6.18
) implies that
|W\setminus\sigma(X_{q+1}^{H})|\leq{\varepsilon}_{T}|W|
.
Together with
\mathbf{S}(q)
(e)
this establishes
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(e)
.

Step 4.6.

Checking
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(f)

Let
(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})\in\mathcal{W}_{set}
be a set tester with
W\subseteq V_{q+1}
and
Y_{j}\subseteq X_{q+1}^{H_{j}}
for all
j\in[\ell]
.
We define

\displaystyle E_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}:=\bigg\{\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell}\}\in\bigsqcup_{j\in[\ell]}E\left(A_{q+1}^{H_{j},q}[W,Y_{j}]\right)\colon\bigcap_{j\in[\ell]}e_{j}\neq\emptyset\bigg\}

and a weight function
\omega_{{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}}\colon\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})}{\ell}\to\{0,1\}
by
\omega_{{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}}(\mathbf{e}):=\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathbf{e}\in E_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}\}}
.
Note that

(6.20)

\displaystyle\textstyle\omega_{{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}}(M)=\big|W\cap\bigcap_{j\in[\ell]}\sigma(Y_{j})\big|.

In view of the statement, we may assume that
|W|,|Y_{j}|\geq{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}n
for all
j\in[\ell]
.
Since
A_{q+1}^{H,q}
is
({\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)},d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)})
-super-regular for every
H\in\mathcal{H}
by
\mathbf{S}(q)
(a)
, we obtain by Fact
3.3
and because
\ell\leq\alpha^{-1}
that there are at most
{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)}^{1/2}n
vertices in
W
that do not have
(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)})d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}|Y_{j}|
neighbours in
Y_{j}
for every
j\in[\ell]
.
Hence, we obtain that

(6.21)

\displaystyle\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))=|E_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}|=(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)})(d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)})^{\ell}|W||Y_{1}|\cdots|Y_{\ell}|.

We check that
\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}
is a local tester:
For all
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell^{\prime}}\}\in\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})}{\ell^{\prime}}
,
\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell]
,
the number of edges
\{e_{\ell^{\prime}+1},\ldots,e_{\ell}\}
such that
\mathbf{e}:=\{e_{j}\}_{j\in[\ell]}\in\binom{E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})}{\ell}
with
\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}(\mathbf{e})
is at most
(2n)^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}
, implying that
\|\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}\|_{\ell^{\prime}}\leq n^{\ell-\ell^{\prime}+{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)}^{2}}
.

Hence,
\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}
is a local tester and we can add
\omega_{E_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}}
to
\mathcal{W}_{local}
.
By
(III)L
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}(M)=(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}^{2})\frac{\omega_{(W,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{\ell})}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))}{(d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n)^{\ell}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:weight W_set}}}{{=}}\frac{|W||Y_{1}|\cdots|Y_{\ell}|}{n^{\ell}}\pm\alpha^{2}n,

which establishes
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(f)
by (
6.20
).

Step 4.7.

Checking
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(g)

In order to establish
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(g)
, let
(\omega_{ver},\mathscrs{c})\in\mathcal{W}_{ver}
be an
\alpha^{-1}
-vertex tester with centres
\mathscrs{c}=\{c_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in V_{\sqcup I}
where
I\subseteq[q+1]
and
q+1\in I
.
By (
6.5
), we defined in particular for all
\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]_{0}^{r}
and
\mathscrs{p}:=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})
a tuple
(\omega_{\iota},J=\emptyset,J_{X}=\emptyset,J_{V}=\emptyset,\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
with initial weight function
\omega_{\iota}=:\omega_{\iota,\mathscrs{p}}
corresponding to
(\omega_{ver},\mathscrs{c})
. That is, by (
6.5
), we have

(6.22)

\displaystyle\omega_{ver}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})=\sum_{\mathscrs{p}\colon\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]_{0}^{r}}\omega_{\iota,\mathscrs{p}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}}).

Note that
\omega_{\iota,\mathscrs{p}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})>0
only if
\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{[q]}\|=\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{[q]}\|
by (
4.5
) since
|I|\leq k-1
.
For
\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]_{0}^{r}
and
\mathscrs{p}:=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})
with
\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{[q]}\|=\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{[q]}\|
, let

\tau{p}:=(\omega_{q},\omega_{\iota},J=\emptyset,J_{X}=\emptyset,J_{V}=\emptyset,\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
be the edge tester with respect to
(\omega_{\iota},J=\emptyset,J_{X}=\emptyset,J_{V}=\emptyset,\mathscrs{c},\mathscrs{p})
,
(\phi_{q},\phi_{q}^{+})
,
\mathcal{A}^{q}
and
\mathcal{B}^{q}
, which is contained in
\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{q}
, and
\omega_{q}\colon E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})\to[0,\alpha^{-1}]
,
and let
\omega_{q,\mathscrs{p}}:=\omega_{q}
.
By
\mathbf{S}(q)
(b)
, we obtain

(6.23)

\displaystyle\omega_{q,\mathscrs{p}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))=(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)})d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}\frac{\omega_{\iota,\mathscrs{p}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|I|-1}}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)}}.

A key observation is that

(6.24)

\displaystyle\omega_{ver}(\phi_{q+1}^{-1}(\mathscrs{c}))=\sum_{\mathscrs{p}\colon\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]_{0}^{r}}\omega_{q,\mathscrs{p}}(M),

which follows from the definition of the edge tester
\tau{p}
for
\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})
as in Definition
4.5
.
Note that
\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}
is
({\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)},d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)})
-super-regular by
\mathbf{S}(q)
(a)
.
For all
\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}\in[k\alpha^{-1}]_{0}^{r}
with
\|\mathbf{p}^{A}_{[q]}\|=\|\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd}_{[q]}\|
and
\mathscrs{p}:=(\mathbf{p}^{A},\mathbf{p}^{A,2nd},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})
, we add
\omega_{q,\mathscrs{p}}\colon E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q})\to[0,\alpha^{-1}]
to
\mathcal{W}_{local}
and obtain by
(III)L
that

\displaystyle\omega_{q,\mathscrs{p}}(M)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{emb lem:local weight}}}{{=}}(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)}^{2})\frac{\omega_{q,\mathscrs{p}}(E(\mathcal{A}_{q+1}^{q}))}{d_{A}^{m_{q+1}(q)}n}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\penalty\ \eqref{eq:weight w_q,p,p}\penalty\ \penalty\ }}{{=}}(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{c(q+1)})\frac{\omega_{\iota,\mathscrs{p}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})}{n^{|I|}}\pm 2n^{{\varepsilon}_{c_{q+1}(q)}}.

Together with (
6.22
) and (
6.24
), this implies that

\displaystyle\omega_{ver}(\phi_{q+1}^{-1}(\mathscrs{c}))=(1\pm{\varepsilon}_{T})\omega_{ver}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup{I}})/n^{|I|}\pm n^{{\varepsilon}_{T}},

which establishes
\mathbf{S}(q+1)
(g)
.

Step 5.

Completion

Let
\phi_{r}\colon\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[r]}\widehat{X}_{i}^{H}\to\mathcal{V}_{r}
be an
r
-partial packing satisfying
\mathbf{S}(r)
with
({\varepsilon}_{T},d_{i})
-super-regular and
({\varepsilon}_{T},t^{2/3})
-well-intersecting candidacy graphs
B_{i}^{H}:=B_{i}^{H,r}\subseteq B_{i}^{H}(\phi_{r}^{+})
where
d_{i}:=d_{B}^{\dg_{R}(i)}=d_{B}^{m_{i}(r)}
for all
i\in[r]
and
\mathcal{X}_{r}^{\phi_{r}}=\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[r]}\widehat{X}_{i}^{H}
.
We will apply a random packing procedure in order to complete the partial packing
\phi_{r}
using the edges in
G_{B}
.
Recall that
{\varepsilon}_{T}\ll\mu\ll\gamma\ll 1/t\ll\alpha,1/k
and we often call the vertices
\bigcup_{H\in\mathcal{H},i\in[r]}(X_{i}^{H}\setminus\widehat{X}_{i}^{H})

unembedded (by
\phi_{r}
)
or the
leftover (of
\phi_{r}
)
.
Our general strategy is as follows.
For every
H\in\mathcal{H}
in turn, we choose a set
Y_{i}^{H}\subseteq\widehat{X}_{i}^{H}
for all
i\in[r]
of size roughly
\mu n
by selecting every vertex uniformly at random with probability
\mu
and adding
X_{i}^{H}\setminus\widehat{X}_{i}^{H}
deterministically.
Afterwards we apply a random matching argument to pack
H[Y^{H}_{\cup[r]}]
into
G_{B}
, which together with
\phi_{r}
yields a complete packing of
H
into
G_{A}\cup G_{B}
.
Before we proceed with the details of our random packing procedure in Claim
6
(Steps
6
–
6
), we verify in Claim
6
(Steps
6
–
6
) that we can indeed pack a subgraph of one single
H\in\mathcal{H}
into
G_{B}
using another random embedding argument as long as our random packing procedure does not deviate too much from its expected behaviour.
To that end, we collect some more notation in Step
6
, and establish several important leftover conditions in Step
6
.

Step 5.1.

Notation for the completion

We introduce some more notation.
We arbitrarily enumerate the graphs in
\mathcal{H}
and write
\mathcal{H}=\{H_{1},\ldots,H_{|\mathcal{H}|}\}
.
For
G^{\circ}\subseteq G_{B}
and
B_{i}^{H}
with
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
i\in[r]
,

(6.25)

let
(B_{i}^{H})^{G^{\circ}}
be the subgraph of
B_{i}^{H}
where
N_{(B_{i}^{H})^{G^{\circ}}}(x)=V_{i}\cap N_{G_{B}-G^{\circ}}(\mathcal{S}_{x})
for every
x\in X_{i}^{H}
,

and
\mathcal{S}_{x}\subseteq\binom{V(G)}{k-1}
is the set such that
N_{B_{i}^{H}}(x)=N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S}_{x})
.
Note that
\mathcal{S}_{x}
exists because
B_{i}^{H}
is
({\varepsilon}_{T},t^{2/3})
-well-intersecting.
This implies in particular that we removed every edge
xv
from
B_{i}^{H}
for which there exists an edge
\mathscrs{e}\in E(H)
such that
\phi_{r}(\mathscrs{e}\setminus\{x\})\cup\{v\}\in E(G^{\circ})
.
We may think of
E(G^{\circ})
as the edge set in
G_{B}
that we have already used in our completion step for packing some other graphs of
\mathcal{H}
into
G_{A}\cup G_{B}
.
Consequently,
(B_{i}^{H})^{G^{\circ}}
is the subgraph of the candidacy graph
B_{i}^{H}
that only contains an edge
xv
if we do not use an edge in
G^{\circ}
when we would map
x
onto
v
.
To count the number of removed edges incident to a vertex
v\in V_{i}
in
B_{i}^{H}
, we define

(6.26)

\displaystyle\rho^{H}_{G^{\circ}}(v):=\big|\big\{x\in N_{B_{i}^{H}}(v)\colon S\cup\{v\}\in E(G^{\circ})\text{ for some }S\in\mathcal{S}_{x}\big\}\big|,

where
\mathcal{S}_{x}\subseteq\binom{V(G)}{k-1}
is the set such that
N_{B_{i}^{H}}(x)=N_{G_{B}}(\mathcal{S}_{x})
.
Note that

(6.27)

\displaystyle\dg_{B_{i}^{H}}(v)-\dg_{(B_{i}^{H})^{G^{\circ}}}(v)=\rho^{H}_{G^{\circ}}(v)\text{ for every $v\in V_{i}$, $i\in[r]$.}

During our random packing procedure we will guarantee that
\rho^{H}_{G^{\circ}}(v)
is negligibly small (Step
6
) and that the probability for a
G_{B}
-edge to be ‘used’ during the completion is appropriately small (at most
\mu^{3/4}
, see Claim
6
).
To that end, we control certain conditions for the leftover of
\phi_{r}
in the next step.

Step 5.2.

Controlling the leftover for the completion

In this step we make some important observations for the completion.
In general, we will employ
\mathbf{S}(r)
(b)
multiple times in order to suitably control the leftover, that is, the structure of the vertices that are left unembedded by
\phi_{r}
.

We start with an observation how to control the number of neighbours of a vertex
v
in
B_{j}^{H}
that are left unembedded by
\phi_{r}
.

Claim 1.

|N_{B_{j}^{H}}(v)\cap(X_{j}^{H}\setminus\widehat{X}_{j}^{H})|\leq 2{\varepsilon}_{T}d_{j}n
for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
j\in[r]
and
v\in V_{j}
.

Proof of claim:
Recall that we defined edge testers in (
6.7
) for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
j\in[r]
and
v\in V_{j}
to count the number of neighbours of
v
in
B_{j}^{H}
that are left unembedded by
\phi_{r}
.
Let
(\omega,\omega_{\iota,H},J=\{j\},J_{X}=\{j\},J_{V}=\emptyset,\mathscrs{c}=\{v\},(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}))
be the edge tester in
\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{r}(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}^{+},\mathcal{A}^{r},\mathcal{B}^{r})
that we obtain from
\mathbf{S}(r)
(b)
.
Definition
4.5
of this edge tester implies that

\displaystyle|N_{B_{j}^{H}}(v)\cap(X_{j}^{H}\setminus\widehat{X}_{j}^{H})|=\omega(E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{r}))=\omega(E(B_{j}^{H}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\text{$\mathbf{S}(r)$\ref{edge weight}}}}}{{\leq}}2{\varepsilon}_{T}d_{j}n.

This establishes Claim
6
.

-

Next, we control the number of neighbours of a vertex
v
in
B_{i}^{H}
that are embedded but lie in an
H
-edge that contains unembedded vertices.

Claim 2.

|N_{B_{i}^{H}}(v)\cap\mathcal{X}_{r}^{\phi_{r}}\cap N_{H_{\ast}}(\mathcal{X}_{r}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{r}^{\phi_{r}})|\leq{\varepsilon}_{T}^{1/2}d_{i}n
for all
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
i\in[r]
and
v\in V_{i}
.

Proof of claim:
Our general strategy is as follows.
We do not only consider a single vertex

but also a second vertex
w\in V_{j}
for some

.
We can use our defined edge testers and employ
\mathbf{S}(r)
(b)
to count for a fixed vertex

Hence, by summing over all possible choices of

Hence, by a double counting argument, for one fixed

In the end, this implies Claim

.
We proceed with the details.

We fix

,

For all
\mathscrs{p}=(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},\mathbf{p}^{B},\mathbf{p}^{B,2nd})

\mathscrs{p}

\omega_{\mathscrs{p}}:=\omega

Note that

\displaystyle\left|N_{B_{i}}(v)\cap\mathcal{X}_{r}^{\phi_{r}}\cap N_{H_{\ast}}(X_{j}^{H}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{r}^{\phi_{r}})\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{{\text{$\mathbf{S}(r)$\ref{edge weight}}}}}}{{\leq}}\left((1-2{\varepsilon}_{T})d_{j}n\right)^{-1}\sum_{w\in V_{j}}\sum_{\mathscrs{p}}\omega{p}\left(E(\mathcal{B}_{i}^{r})\sqcup E(\mathcal{B}_{j}^{r})\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\text{$\mathbf{S}(r)$\ref{edge weight}}}}}{{\leq}}\left((1-2{\varepsilon}_{T})d_{j}n\right)^{-1}2n\sum_{\mathscrs{p}}\left({\varepsilon}_{T}d_{i}d_{j}\omega_{\iota,\mathscrs{p}}(\mathscr{X}_{\sqcup J})+n^{{\varepsilon}_{T}}\right)

.

Claim 3.

\left|E_{\phi_{r}}(\mathscrs{g},J,J_{X},J_{V})\right|\leq\left(\mathbbm{1}\{J_{XV}=\emptyset\}+2{\varepsilon}_{T}\right)\gamma^{-1}n^{|J|}\prod_{j\in J}d_{j}

Proof of claim:

\mathscrs{r}=I
,

,

(\omega,\omega_{\iota},J,J_{X},J_{V},\mathscrs{g},\mathscrs{p})

\mathcal{W}_{edge}^{r}(\phi_{r},\phi_{r}^{+},\mathcal{A}^{r},\mathcal{B}^{r})

In order to be able to distinguish these edge testers according to the patterns

Note that for

By Definition

of an edge tester and by summing over all patterns

Note that the properties

–

–

Due to

of Definition

additionally requires for an element

That is, elements

where some vertices

are already embedded by

onto
